campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:19: **A note on the Zhentarim.** Two Zhentarim cells operate in Waterdeep. Davil Starsong's **Doom Raiders** are the player-joinable faction covered in the **Organizations** pages. Manshoon's faction is the villain cell, which the faction-mission journals call **the Splinter**. They are hostile to the Doom Raiders and to the party.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:46: | [10 Manshoon's Zhentarim](10-manshoons-zhentarim.md) | Response Teams, Outposts, Escalation, Revelation List |
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:11: - That the Waterdeep Harper network has been infiltrated by double-agents working for the other half of the Black Network (after Mission 4, if the party has met Davil)
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:19: The Splinter's infiltration of the Waterdeep cell creates complications organically. The Harpers know only that the Black Network has split and that the Splinter acts on Harper information. Anything the party shares with Harper contacts can reach the Splinter, which rewards paranoid operational security.
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:22: > The Splinter has planted at least one double-agent in the Waterdeep Harper cell, exposed during **The Sleeping Asset**. Intelligence the party discloses to Harper contacts before that mission may reach the Splinter. Track what has been shared and when, because foreknowledge of Harper-held information is among the Splinter's most effective advantages. The Harpers do not know that the Splinter is Manshoon's cell and answers to Kolat Towers. That is GM knowledge, and no Harper contact names him.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:13: Alliance speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener. Jalester also separates the Splinter from Davil Starsong's cell, with the words "not Davil's people". They say "the carnival fleet" until **Jarlaxle Unmasked** is marked. They never say more of the Cassalanters than that the family is too rich and too pleasant, and nobody in the Alliance has proof of anything.
campaign/guides/factions/04-emerald-enclave.md:13: Enclave speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener. Kolat Towers stands one block north of Phaulkonmere, and nobody in the Enclave points at it before that gate. They never name the Cassalanters as anything worse than a rich house, and they never say "Jarlaxle" or "Bregan D'aerthe" to players.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:13: Order speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener, and Savra keeps Davil's Doom Raiders apart from the Splinter. They say "the carnival fleet" until **Jarlaxle Unmasked** is marked. Savra says "the family" until a player names the Cassalanters, and then she adds that she has no proof. Nobody in the Order knows what the family worships, and nobody names a pact.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:7: Vajra's mandate is the city, and in particular the parts that arcane threats touch. At campaign start she knows that magical coercion is spreading among Waterdhavians in positions of influence, and she suspects a link to the gang war. She knows the Black Network has split. Hlam's first message gives her only the shape of the old Black Network coming back, with no name attached. She doesn't yet know the Stone of Golorr has resurfaced, or that four factions are competing for the vault.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:11: Force Grey speakers say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener, and they name Manshoon and Kolat Towers only after that. They never say "Jarlaxle" or "Bregan D'aerthe" to players.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:70: | Joined, 2nd level | **Consulting Hlam** | Climb to the hermit monk Hlam's cave on Mount Waterdeep and ask what threats he has perceived. He answers honest visitors, and his second answer is a riddle about the old Black Network coming back. | +2 |
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:75: | Renown 13, 7th level | **Smoke in the Tower** | Splinter survivors strike Blackstaff Tower after **Kolat Towers**, using a resonance disruptor that Vira Solkan hid in the Tower. Who sent them depends on what Kolat Towers left of Manshoon, and no speaker names him until **Manshoon Named** is marked. Find Vira, hold the north service door and record a prisoner's testimony. The reward is the Open Lord's recognition, with no rank. | +4 |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:1: # The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:3: Game mechanics for running the Doom Raiders (Davil Starsong's Zhentarim cell) as a player faction. For who they are, see the **Doom Raiders** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:7: The Doom Raiders are the legitimate Black Network in Waterdeep, and the faction is at war with itself. Davil's cell arrived in Waterdeep first. Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter came later as a rival cell, peeled off part of that network, and now works to dismantle Davil's people and everything they built. This internal conflict is the Doom Raiders' defining preoccupation. The Grand Game is secondary.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:9: The half-million dragons are another matter. Efforts are underway to reclaim and rebuild Zhentil Keep, and the reforming Zhentarim Council is choosing its membership. That money could buy Davil's cell a seat at that table — genuine continental influence rather than the precarious position of a regional branch operation. Characters who recover the gold and deliver it to the Doom Raiders are buying their patrons into something much larger than one campaign.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:11: The Doom Raiders do not tell the party until Mission 4 forces the truth out. **Skeemo Weirdbottle has been a traitor from the start.** The Master of Magic has been feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell and to House Gralhund since before the party was recruited. Everything the party has done for the faction has been visible to the Splinter. Davil has suspected something for months. Tashlyn confirmed it during his arrest.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:19: - Anything the party learns about Manshoon's cell: safe house locations, lieutenant identities, communication methods, operational timing. One confirmed piece per tenday is worth more than a dramatic report filed once a month.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:23: Davil's ideal outcome: the vault gold funds the Zhentarim Council bid, Manshoon's cell is dismantled before it can contest the vault, Skeemo's betrayal is cleaned up internally without Watch involvement, and Ziraj is alive. He keeps the moral ledger as balanced as his business allows.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:30: | **Gralhund Villa** | Davil is arrested in the Watch's post-Gralhund crackdown on all Zhentarim operations. A flying snake delivers the first word: *"Davil is unavailable. Continue normal operations. Await further contact."* Tashlyn takes over. Her first direct message to the party: *"Keep a low profile. I'll be in touch."* She will not explain what that means. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:31: | **Faction Outposts** | The Doom Raiders have been watching Yellowspire, Manshoon's Castle Ward relay point, for two months. Tashlyn has schematics. She has been waiting for someone capable of going inside. Mission 5 is the operational payoff of that patience. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:33: | **Vault of Dragons** | At Dread Lord renown: Davil proposes a 10% split in exchange for a distraction crew, a locksmith backup, and a wagon extraction team. If Skeemo is still alive, he is present at the vault working for Manshoon's simulacrum and will recognize the party on sight. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:45: - **Feed intelligence on Manshoon's cell to Davil or Tashlyn (+1).** The internal war is the Doom Raiders' most pressing problem. Any confirmed information about Manshoon's agents, safe houses, or operational plans earns this reward. One award per distinct piece of intelligence.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:46: - **Neutralize a named Manshoon operative (+2).** Eliminate or capture one of Manshoon's lieutenants. Delivering a prisoner to Tashlyn earns the full renown; confirmed elimination earns +1.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:47: - **Protect a Doom Raider commercial front from disruption (+1).** If a Black Network legitimate business is targeted by Manshoon's agents, the Watch, or a rival faction, characters who intervene earn renown. Once per incident.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:57: | 3 | **Wolf** | Davil or Tashlyn provides one piece of actionable intelligence on a rival faction's activities per tenday, drawn from the Network's informant web. Restricted goods available at a 20% discount through the supply chain, including thieves' tools, forgery kits, disguise kits, poisons, and unlicensed weapons. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:59: | 25 | **Ardragon** | The Network deploys a three-person crew (two **spies** and one **assassin**) to assist in one heist or operation per quest. Davil's informants inside Manshoon's cell can be activated for specific intelligence once per quest (DC 14 Intelligence check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two tendays). Ziraj the Hunter extends Davil's friends a favor: each Ardragon member can request it once per campaign, and one member's use does not spend another's. Ziraj neutralizes one named target, captured alive or quietly eliminated, and won't take assignments he considers suicidal. If Ziraj dies, Yagra Stonefist offers a capture-only substitute in its place. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:60: | 50 | **Dread Lord** | Eight Black Network operatives are available for one major operation. Davil proposes the character for a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council, a position of genuine continental influence within the Black Network. Doom Raiders safe houses in every ward. Davil offers formal partnership for the vault operation: a distraction crew draws off one faction's forces, a locksmith backup stands by, and a wagon team handles extraction logistics, in exchange for 10% of whatever the party walks away with. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:70: | 6th | **The Yellowspire Job** | Infiltrate Yellowspire, Manshoon's Castle Ward relay tower, and steal the pass-amulet distribution ledger showing which lieutenants carry which amulets on what rotation — the key to bypassing Kolat Towers' force field. | +4 |

campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:73: **Two days after Gralhund Villa, the City Watch arrests Davil and holds him at Castle Waterdeep pending questioning about the Zhentarim's role in the street violence.** Contact passes to Tashlyn Yafeera during his detention; see the [**Davil's Arrest**](../../quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/ev-01-davils-arrest.md) Event. Davil is released at the end of Mission 4, once the Lords conclude his cell isn't responsible for the recent killings; see the [**Davil's Return**](../../quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s02-davils-return/ev-01-davils-return.md) Event.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:9: Rather than competing with Xanathar or Manshoon for the Stone, Jarlaxle already holds Eye #3, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* and mounted beneath the *Eyecatcher*'s keel, and is content to let the other factions exhaust themselves chasing each other. His asset inside Xanathar's organization — **Nar'l Xibrindas**, a drow mage who serves as the beholder's advisor and reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell — keeps him informed of the Guild's movements. The Sea Maidens Faire parades give him cover to move personnel and materials through any ward without scrutiny.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:90: - A surveillance report from Fel'rekt Lafeen on the Gralhund Villa observation post, noting Zhentarim presence and gazer activity nearby.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:130: - *A Directive to Zorbog* (Zhentarim Interrogation House — mentions Fenerus by name as a D'aerthe contact)

campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:1: # Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:3: Game mechanics for running Manshoon's Zhentarim as a villain faction. For who they are, see the **Manshoon's Zhentarim** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:7: All three Zhentarim response teams consist of a lieutenant plus 3–4 **thugs**. Every lieutenant carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers and will try to destroy it if capture seems certain, except Agorn, who trades his to save himself.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:9: Any Zhentarim team sent to attack the characters carries orders to take them as prisoners to the Interrogation House on Brindul Alley. Questioned team members can point the characters toward the house. The lighter roster is intentional. Manshoon wants intelligence, not body counts, and underestimated enemies are more useful to him than dead ones. These teams also keep pressure on the party without forcing a lethal confrontation.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:19: *Independent discovery path: parties that searched Urstul Floxin's effects at Gralhund Villa have documents pointing to Brindul Alley. Parties that did not can find the safe house through North Ward canvassing — the traffic is unusual enough to notice (DC 14 Investigation over a day, no faction membership required). This is the Manshoon chain's street-level entry point.*
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:21: Use the map for house L1 (DH p. 62). Avareen Windrivver and Zorbog Jyarkoth maintain this building as a combination safe house and covert interrogation center. Three black **flying snakes** nest near the ceiling — the Zhentarim's message couriers.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:23: *Front Room.* Avareen and Zorbog's quarters. Avareen carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers. While the party explores, a flying snake arrives with *A Directive to Zorbog* signed by "Manshoon," ordering Zorbog to kidnap Fenerus Stormcastle of Quill Alley, a "known associate of these new meddlers in the Grand Game."
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:25: *Backroom.* A rack and other instruments of torture. An arcane circle on the floor. Strapped into the rack is **Ott Steeltoes**, a Xanathar's Guild agent captured while the Zhentarim were trying to locate the Eye. Ott put the Eye in Sylgar's bowl as a pretty stone and has no idea what it is.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:31: **Escalation:** Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3). Flying snakes carry the alarm within hours if any escape; Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 3 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:37: Use the Winter version of the Old Tower (DH p. 79). Amath Sercent and four **acolytes** occupy this converted tower. The permanent teleportation circle here connects directly to Kolat Towers — one of the few physical routes into Manshoon's lair that bypasses the pass-amulet requirement.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:39: *Area O2 — Ground Level.* A crumpled note from Avareen to Sercent, full of barely contained contempt: Avareen complains about Manshoon's directive to hand over a broadsheet publisher named Shan Chien for Sercent's "foul rites," but closes with "Manshoon's word is my command."
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:45: **Escalation:** Manshoon moves to Suspicious (Tier 2) if the Interrogation House has not yet been hit; moves to Lockdown (Tier 4) if it has. Agorn carries word to surviving Zhentarim contacts if he escapes; response teams redeploy within 3 days. **Founders' Day Clock: 3–5 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:54: | Lockdown | Urstul's team if the others fail; Manshoon issues personal directives by flying snake. Manshoon's simulacrum engages; Sidra locks down Kolat Towers |
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:56: Manshoon underestimates threats that aren't magical. A party that stays clear of Kolat Towers may go tendays before he escalates beyond his response teams. His preference for capturing over killing creates tactical openings.
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:62: **Zhentarim Interrogation House** (North Ward, Brindul Alley)
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:65: - Questioning Zhentarim response teams / being captured by them
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:82: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim** organization page
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:85: - **Manshoon** villain page
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:9: **All four villain factions run simultaneously.** No season villain. Xanathar's Guild, Manshoon's Zhentarim, the Cassalanters, and Bregan D'aerthe are active from session one, each pursuing a different angle on the same prize.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:13: **The three Eyes are distributed among the factions.** Recovering all three from their holders is the precondition for opening the vault. Kolat Towers, Manshoon's fortress, holds no Eye.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:32: **Feature All Villains** means keeping all four factions moving between sessions. Faction Response Teams deploy when the party hits assets; the party's choices shift which factions gain ground. Each faction responds to threat differently: Xanathar escalates on instinct, Manshoon prefers capture over kill, the Cassalanters exhaust legal and social leverage before calling up infernal muscle. That difference should be legible at the table. See [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) for the between-session tracking procedure.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:48: The MacGuffin chain runs in a straight line: the Stone of Golorr names the vault but can't open it without all three Eyes. Xanathar holds Eye #1 (in Sylgar's fish bowl, unknowingly). The Cassalanters hold Eye #2. Jarlaxle holds Eye #3 aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Kolat Towers holds Manshoon, not an Eye. It's the fourth heist target; clearing it before the Vault pushes the party to Level 7.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:29: Five quests, four heist targets, no fixed order. The party holds the Stone and needs three Eyes to open the Vault. Faction Outposts provides the intelligence to plan. Three of the four lair heists hold an Eye; Kolat Towers holds Manshoon. Each target is built as a genuine operation: scoutable, plannable, and responsive to how thoroughly the party prepares. Xanathar's Lair is a dungeon infiltration into the criminal undercity. Cassalanter Villa is social penetration into a noble household with a cult temple beneath it. The Sea Maidens Faire is a nautical caper aboard three ships. Kolat Towers is a fortress assault against Manshoon's extradimensional sanctum. Two clocks run underneath all of it: the Faire departs Tarsakh 20 and Founders' Day falls Flamerule 1. Act III is the bulk of the campaign. Thorough planning pays off; the structure supports improvisation.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:53: ### The Holder: Urstul Floxin (Manshoon's Zhentarim)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:55: Urstul Floxin is Manshoon's chief field operative in Waterdeep: a large, heavyset Illuskan man in his forties who moves through rooms as though he owns them and reserves his anger for moments when it will accomplish something. He is methodical, personally dangerous, and one of the more capable people the character will encounter during the campaign.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:62: > Floxin works for Manshoon, not the Doom Raiders. A character whose Debt is held by Floxin is already entangled with the campaign's primary villain faction, whether they know it or not. Davil Starsong's cell is a separate organization and won't automatically come to this character's aid. Let the character discover the distinction on their own.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:133: He's a genuine professional who believes the Doom Raiders' moderate approach to crime is both more effective and more defensible than Manshoon's methods. His first formal ask of the character is small and framed as exactly that: he's calibrating them, not exploiting them, and he says so. Characters who engage honestly find the relationship evolving into something closer to a working partnership over the course of the campaign. Characters who attempt to use the asset-running as leverage against him will find that Davil has been anticipating that move since before the first note was dropped.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:15: The remix runs all four simultaneously for three reasons. First, a city where four powerful factions are genuinely competing gives players organic reasons to act without the plot needing to push them. If the Cassalanters are poisoning people, Xanathar is having informants killed, and Manshoon is blackmailing city officials, the party doesn't need a quest to care. Second, four-faction play creates genuine choice: which lair do you hit first? Whose Eye do you trade for an alliance? A single-villain campaign has only one answer to those questions. Third, no single villain defines the campaign's identity, which means it can accommodate different playstyles at once. The group that wants political intrigue gets it through the Cassalanters; the group that wants dungeon delving gets it through Xanathar's Lair.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:27: The four lairs run as four distinct genres. Xanathar's Lair is dungeon infiltration: break into an underground complex with a beholder at the center. Cassalanter Villa is social infiltration: work within a functioning household whose owners are watching you back. Sea Maidens Faire is a caper: the thing that's supposed to go wrong goes wrong in the first act, and you adapt in real time. Kolat Towers is a raid: Manshoon isn't a puzzle, he's an obstacle, and the question is whether you can take him. Different player instincts dominate different genres, so the heist phase plays differently from session to session rather than feeling like four iterations of the same map.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:125: - Vell (Manshoon Splinter spy; **The Talking Mare** antagonist hunting Maxeene) — single-mission NPC; no profile
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:43: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the party spends three sessions pursuing Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon haven't been idle. Keep a one-line answer ready for what each faction did during that time. When PCs canvass contacts, read Watch reports, or press informants, they may turn up traces of that off-screen activity. The world feels inhabited rather than scripted when the factions are visibly busy with their own agendas.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:51: **Manshoon** underestimates threats that aren't magical. A party that avoids Kolat Towers and doesn't advertise itself through arcane channels can operate at Suspicious tier for multiple sessions before he escalates. His preference for capturing over killing creates tactical openings, since a confrontation often produces a job offer before a fight.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:61: Manshoon's Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild are in active conflict at street level in the Dock Ward. Manshoon's operatives probe Guild territory regularly; Xanathar has issued a kill order on Manshoon that has failed twice. This is a grinding low-level engagement that produces casualties on both sides and keeps both factions consuming resources on a front entirely separate from the PCs.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:65: Jarlaxle and Manshoon maintain a fragile non-interference arrangement. Each considers the other the only genuine peer in the Grand Game, and neither wants direct conflict while other factions are more immediate problems. The arrangement is under strain: Manshoon has identified Fenerus Stormcastle as a Bregan D'aerthe cut-out and ordered his abduction. If that abduction proceeds, Jarlaxle will act unilaterally. That is a warning step. The non-interference ends in any case when Manshoon's strike team hits the *Eyecatcher* at **Sea Maidens Faire**, a strike that always fires.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:72: > This is the Grand Game's self-reinforcing pressure. If the PCs dismantle Xanathar's Guild outposts, Manshoon's Zhentarim moves into the vacated Dock Ward territory. If the PCs weaken Manshoon, Xanathar's paranoia briefly lifts and it turns outward. The PCs' choices produce real chain reactions because thinking factions respond to changing conditions. The inter-faction operations matrix in **Running the Villains** shows where each faction's active operations overlap and which documents cross faction lines; consult it whenever the party clears territory a rival faction would want.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:78: **The Doom Raiders** are a player-joinable faction, not a villain faction. If the party has turned them hostile, Davil Starsong will negotiate the moment he sees a path to de-escalation. What he wants is information: something useful about the party's other activities or contacts. A working relationship can be bought back with the right currency. Turning the Doom Raiders hostile closes the Zhentarim renown track and the cell's contact network; restoring goodwill reopens them.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:80: **Manshoon** can be satisfied in the short term by a demonstration of usefulness or the delivery of an asset he values. His preference for capturing over killing means a direct confrontation often produces a job offer before violence, at least until the party has cost him too much. Manshoon holds debts. A party that delivers something useful will find him willing to deal again.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:99: - [Setting: Villains](../../setting/villains/) — personality phases and goals for Xanathar, Manshoon, the Cassalanters, and Jarlaxle
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:102: - [Manshoon's Zhentarim](../factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md) — response teams
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:14: | Doom Raiders (Zhentarim) | Criminal network access, fences, safe houses, street intelligence | Jobs that keep the network profitable |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:18: > If you want your character to join "the Zhentarim," you're joining the **Doom Raiders**. A separate Zhentarim cell operates elsewhere in the city, answering to a different and far more dangerous leader, with different contacts, different methods, and a moral code that has nothing in common with the Doom Raiders'. The two organizations do not get along, and your GM will always make clear which one you're dealing with.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:63: - Prevent large-scale violence, assist the City Watch beyond their capacity, or counter Zhentarim Splinter operations.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:70: | **Warduke** | 25 | Once per quest, four Warrior Veterans from the Watch fight beside you in one operation on the surface. A sheet on the villain factions' response teams waits at the Portal's drop every third day. You can ask the Open Lord for one private audience per quest. Once per quest, a written request through your contact asks a Masked Lord, whom your contact will not name, to open a door, freeze an inquiry or apply political pressure. The Veterans fight as one fewer than the party, to a cap of four, and the six Alliance missions cannot use them. The sheet names a team only after the member has met it, and it says "the Splinter" and "the carnival fleet" until **Manshoon Named** and **Jarlaxle Unmasked** are marked. The Masked Lord is never named and is never Mirt, and Jalester does not know who it is. Laeral receives the member at the Palace the morning after the rank is named. A character who lies to her at an audience must beat a DC 16 Charisma (Deception) check. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:83: | **Summerstrider** | 3 | Once per tenday, Melannor sends a message of up to 25 words for you by *animal messenger*. Jeryth heals injuries you took on Enclave business, in the garden at Phaulkonmere, once per Long Rest. Once per ward per tenday, Melannor gives you a report from the Enclave's network of watchers. The message goes to a place Melannor has visited and carries a reply only if the message asks for one. It never reaches Undermountain. Jeryth casts *cure wounds*, *healing word*, *lesser restoration* or *protection from poison* at its lowest level, for the member only. The reports are fixed text. The Trades Ward report is closed until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the member, and the Field Ward report reads **Splinter Site Reported**. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:122: ### Doom Raiders (Zhentarim)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:124: The Doom Raiders reward intelligence on their internal enemies and protection of the Network's commercial fronts. The internal war with a rival Zhentarim cell makes every confirmed piece of intelligence about that cell valuable.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:132: | **Wolf** | 3 | Your contact provides one piece of actionable intelligence on a rival faction per tenday, drawn from the Network's informant web. Access to a Black Network safe house in the Dock Ward. Restricted goods (thieves' tools, forgery kits, disguise kits, poisons, unlicensed weapons) available through the supply chain at a 20% discount. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:135: | **Dread Lord** | 50 | Eight Black Network operatives for one major operation. Your contact proposes you for a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council. Doom Raiders safe houses in every ward. The faction offers an end-game operational partnership: a distraction crew, a locksmith backup, and a wagon extraction team, in exchange for 10% of whatever you recover. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:163: | Doom Raiders | **Davil Starsong** (Missions 1–2); **Tashlyn Yafeera** (Missions 3–6) | Intelligence on Manshoon's cell; containment of Xanathar's Guild activity; anything that weakens their rivals |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:190: The factions' interests are incompatible by design. The Lords' Alliance wants the gold returned to the city treasury intact. The Doom Raiders want Manshoon destroyed; his cell threatens their entire operation. Force Grey wants the Grand Game resolved before it spills into public view and embarrasses the city. Bregan D'aerthe wants a political favor that extends well beyond Waterdeep's walls.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:192: A party with members in multiple factions will face these tensions as individual character obligations. When a Harper PC and a Doom Raider PC disagree about whether to hand the Stone to Mirt or protect Manshoon's rival operation, that's a character moment. Let it play as one. Treat each faction position as a real constraint on that character, give the players space to negotiate it between themselves, and resist resolving it by erasing one character's obligation. The disagreement is the texture of the campaign's second act.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:200: The seven factions above are the organizations available for player characters to join. The other major powers active in Waterdeep during the Grand Game — **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter**, and the **Cassalanters** — are adversary factions. Players may interact with, negotiate with, or broker deals with these organizations, but formal membership is not available through normal play. See **Notable Figures** for details on their leaders and agents, and **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for how to run them at the table.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:21: **Eye #1** — formerly held by Manshoon's Zhentarim, who stole it from Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. Xanathar killed the Zhentarim envoys who brought it to him and seized the Eye. It now resides in Xanathar's Lair sanctum.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:25: **Eye #3** — held by Jarlaxle Baenre, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. It began in Renaer Neverember's mourning locket. Manshoon's Zhentarim seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse, Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried off the Eye with the rest of the back office without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the same night. It reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout, and Manshoon's cell never held it. Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep. He is active from Act I through his personas — J.B. Nevercott in Trollskull Alley and Zardoz Zord at the Field of Triumph — and his villain escalation keys off the **Jarlaxle Informed** Event Outcome set during **Fireball!**, which records only that the party made contact with him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:30: > **Eye #3:** Jarlaxle Baenre — aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Jarlaxle knows exactly what it is. It passed from Renaer's locket to the Zhentarim, then to Xanathar's Guild in the warehouse raid, and Bregan D'aerthe agents inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it that night. Manshoon holds no Eye.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:39: | Manshoon's Zhentarim | The Stone exists and unlocks great wealth; Urstul Floxin's team was tracking Dalakhar; does not yet know the Stone reached the Gralhunds | Nothing | Stone + all three Eyes |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:50: | **Manshoon's Zhentarim** | Normal operations | Flying snake courier network active; Agorn Fuoco and Avareen Windrivver file field reports. Urstul Floxin reviews them | Agorn Fuoco or Vevette Blackwater's team; orders are to capture, not kill | Urstul Floxin's team if the others fail; Manshoon issues personal directives by flying snake. Manshoon's simulacrum engages; Sidra Romeir locks down Kolat Towers |

campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:53: **The two Zhentarims.** In Waterdeep, "the Zhentarim" describes two separate organizations with nothing in common except the name. Davil Starsong's Doom Raiders are criminals who present as legitimate businesspeople and will recruit the party during Trollskull Alley; they're a joinable player faction. A rival Zhentarim cell operates elsewhere in the city, answers to a different and far more dangerous leader, and is openly hostile to the Doom Raiders. Establish this at session zero; it's the distinction most likely to confuse players once the city's faction dynamics become visible. Joining the Doom Raiders doesn't mean working for the campaign's primary villain faction.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:5: The campaign contains two distinct Zhentarim factions. Conflating them is a preparation error with real consequences.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:7: **The Doom Raiders** are Davil Starsong's cell — a player-joinable faction that presents a public face of legitimate business and moderate criminal enterprise. They operate openly, hold meetings in taverns, and recruit adventurers. They don't acknowledge Manshoon's cell as legitimate Zhentarim, and they surface during **Trollskull Alley** as the first Zhentarim contact the party encounters.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:9: **Manshoon's Zhentarim** are the villain faction. They operate from Kolat Towers, conduct assassinations, and maintain mind-controlled servants. Manshoon regards the Doom Raiders as deserters who stole his name.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:11: These two cells won't cooperate. If their operations overlap, they actively undermine each other. When a scene involves "the Zhentarim," know which cell is present. The distinction should become apparent to attentive players during **Trollskull Alley** — earlier encounters with the Doom Raiders' professionalism make Manshoon's operatives land harder when they appear.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:14: > Every scene involving a "Zhentarim" NPC requires knowing which cell is present. The Doom Raiders and Manshoon's Zhentarim won't coordinate or share intelligence. If their operations run parallel, they'll try to disrupt each other. Davil Starsong's renown mechanic is entirely separate from Manshoon's faction state. Track them separately and never conflate the two when reading Event Outcomes.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:26: **Team personalities.** Full rosters are on the Factions guide pages ([Bregan D'aerthe](../factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md), [Xanathar's Guild](../factions/09-xanathars-guild.md), [Manshoon's Zhentarim](../factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md), [Cassalanters](../factions/11-cassalanters.md)). Their tactical characters at the table:
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:29: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim** — professional mercenary tactics; more willing to negotiate mid-fight than any other faction; there are always more members nearby than visible.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:90: Verification lives in the Revelation Lists on the villain Factions guide pages ([Bregan D'aerthe](../factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md), [Xanathar's Guild](../factions/09-xanathars-guild.md), [Manshoon's Zhentarim](../factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md), [Cassalanters](../factions/11-cassalanters.md)).

Rapiers and daggers lie nearby. The fight was no more than a day old.

campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/z02-storage-closet.md:14: - Five Zhentarim grabbed him and Floon outside Old Xoblob Shop last night

Nothing of value. The sandals and cloth predate the Zhentarim occupation.
campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/z05-offices.md:7: **Paper bird (magic item):** On the reception desk, folded from a torn sheet of vellum — a message that never left. DC 14 Intelligence (Arcana) identifies it as a *paper bird* (WDH Appendix A magic item: when a command word is spoken, a name is written on the bird in ink, and it flies to find that person, delivering a written message of up to twenty-five words). The bird is currently blank. The command word is penciled on the underside of the desk: *Shan* (a Zhentarim courier alias).
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:9: ***The Two Zhentarims in Finding Floon.*** Dragon Heist presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Finding Floon to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist (a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people) is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar's Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Trollskull Alley and then raid Kolat Towers in Kolat Towers will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1: one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. Players feel the distinction before anyone explains it.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:21: ***Renaer's Introduction.*** In the source, finding Renaer in the warehouse is a pleasant surprise in an otherwise thin scene (a friendly NPC who points the party downward). This treatment makes Renaer's introduction structural: the warehouse is the quest's pivot. The party arrives expecting Floon and finds someone who matters more to the campaign's back story. Renaer's knowledge state is asymmetric by design. He knows the Zhentarim took his locket and that the Guild attacked before they finished with him, without knowing what the locket contained or where it went. His ignorance is genuine. This positions him as a trustworthy ally (he doesn't conceal what he knows) while preserving the campaign's central mystery (the nature of the Eyes, the Vault's location). The gap between what they'll eventually learn and what Renaer knows gives the warehouse scene a retrospective weight absent from the source text.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:105: > Doom Raiders: Yagra, flying snake tattoo on her neck, Zhentarim pendant worn openly. Xanathar's Guild: Krentz and four thugs with eye-shaped tattoos shaved into scalps. Bregan D'aerthe: Ryvarra at the balcony table. Manshoon's Zhentarim: not present at the Yawning Portal. The Dock Ward killing Yagra referenced is their work.

campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/overview.md:50: The Dock Ward investigation produced three independent paths to the perpetrators' identity and four to the warehouse address on Candle Lane. En route, they crossed a City Watch crime scene where Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim thugs were under arrest — the aftermath of the same kidnapping that took Floon. The investigation established both Revelation #1 (who took Floon) and Revelation #2 (where to find them) before they reached the warehouse gate.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/overview.md:52: Inside the warehouse, a Xanathar's Guild raid had already wiped out Manshoon's cell. Four kenku were searching the wreckage. Floon wasn't there. The man they found was Renaer Neverember, the former Open Lord's estranged son, grabbed by mistake and hidden in a back room when the raid hit. Renaer explained that Floon had been mistaken for him, dragged unconscious through a cellar trapdoor into the sewer below, and taken to a Xanathar's Guild hideout following yellow chalk guildsigns. Three distinct leads confirmed the route.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:49: **The Most Trollskull Alley-Specific Faction Content.** Force Grey Missions 1 and 2 are the only Level 2 faction missions designed primarily to feed information threads rather than build renown in isolation. Hlam's warning (Mission 1) gives the first specific reference to Manshoon by implication. Zelifarn's barnacle-covered shipwreck lair at the harbor bottom (Mission 2) contains physical evidence of unusual deep-harbor activity pointing toward Skullport. Force Grey is the only faction where the Level 2–3 missions function as intelligence operations. Parties who join Force Grey in Trollskull Alley arrive at Xanathar's Lair with more operational context than any other faction affiliation provides.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-03-the-neighbors.md:59: > - **Two Zhentarims Known** — mark when Fala mentions Ziraj, or when a follow-up conversation about the "friend who visits the second floor" takes place. It shows the Doom Raiders' personal-connection style before Davil Starsong's invitation arrives, so the difference between the Doom Raiders and Manshoon's Splinter is easier to absorb. Read by **The Factions Come Calling** (ev-04), as context for Doom Raiders enrollment.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-04-the-factions-come-calling.md:82: When the party joins the Doom Raiders or receives a direct briefing from Davil Starsong, the Doom Raiders / Manshoon's Splinter distinction is made explicit. Three paths to this clarification:
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-04-the-factions-come-calling.md:85: 2. Yagra Stonefist as staff candidate (the **Trollskull Manor** guide) — her Zhentarim pendant and Doom Raiders affiliation visible to any PC who asks
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:21: ***Floxin as Self-Serving Witness.*** His cooperation is not generosity. He wants the Stone tracked so the Zhentarim can follow. His account of the "metal man" is accurate because a false description produces a false lead and he needs the real one found. Parties that pass DC 14 Insight must decide what to do with a useful informant who is also a tail.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:61: ***The First Quest Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Finding Floon establishes the Guild and the Zhentarim as adversaries. Trollskull Alley introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and BD as observers. Fireball is the first quest where all four reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor — they are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:175: When the party mentions Fala's (or Tally's) account of the burned man fleeing north, Renaer reaches out to Harper contacts at once. Within one day (the morning of Ches 23rd at the latest), he returns: the burned man is **Urstul Floxin**, Manshoon's chief field agent in Waterdeep. A North Ward resident saw Floxin — visibly burned and limping — enter Gralhund Villa on Saerdoun Street within hours of the blast. The City Watch sent two constables who were told everything was fine. No warrant was issued.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:184: > **Path C — Speak with Dead, the Zhentarim.** Bashekk Ortallis and Wern Malkrave each give five answers. Combined: they were tracking Dalakhar for Floxin because whatever he carried would make them "as rich as kings." Independent confirmation of both the pursuit and the object's value.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:200: **Doom Raiders — Davil Starsong (Renown 1+):** Davil is still free during Fireball. He identifies Floxin immediately as Manshoon's blade. He suspects Floxin filed an anonymous Watch tip naming the Doom Raiders as persons of interest — damage control meant to draw Watch heat away from the Splinter cell. He wants proof of Floxin's direct involvement. He also wants to know if anyone arrived at the blast site early — before the Watch, before the other witnesses — and behaved in a way that suggested foreknowledge. He has a suspicion he is not ready to name.

Notes that Harper intelligence in this district may be tainted, because the other half of the Black Network seems to hear what the Harpers learn. He says this in a low voice and does not elaborate.

campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:35: ## Manshoon Stays Invisible
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:37: The party fights Manshoon's operatives throughout this quest and does not learn whose operatives they are. Floxin does not name his master. This is consistent with his Notable Figures profile and with how operational security works in a cell structure where the operator never sees the principal. DC 14 Insight during any interrogation of Floxin confirms this genuinely: he knows a dead-drop chain and a code, not a name or a face.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:39: When the Manshoon reveal comes, it needs to land as a recontextualization of events the party has already lived through. A **Gralhund Villa** reveal produces a name they file away. A later reveal, after they have encountered his agents in multiple quests without connecting them to a single source, produces the retroactive click that defines a well-structured mystery.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:45: If the chase fails and the Stone reaches a faction safe house, the campaign continues from a weaker position. Jarlaxle will deal with the party. Xanathar will not. Manshoon's cell now knows the party is active and failed. These consequences reshape the social landscape of subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is **Faction Outposts**' problem, not this quest's.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:57: Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's North Ward operations become more careful. Jarlaxle leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown (who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn) define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-01-what-the-factions-say.md:26: The most direct voice in the room. Davil places Floxin in Trollskull Alley that night and names him as the reason Dalakhar is dead and the Stone is behind a locked gate in the North Ward. He frames recovery as a matter of clearing the Doom Raiders' name: the Watch has witnesses who placed Zhentarim agents in the alley. What the Watch doesn't have is anyone willing to say those agents weren't working for the Doom Raiders. Floxin's testimony — his own account of who sent him into Trollskull Alley and why — can separate the Doom Raiders from the bombing entirely.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-01-what-the-factions-say.md:77: The source who named the villa does not have a faction brief to deliver. He wants to understand what his father's vault has to do with the Gralhunds, and what the Gralhunds have to do with the Zhentarim who were chasing Dalakhar through Trollskull Alley the night of the explosion. His access to Harper contacts gave the party the address. His family history gives him a stake in what happens once they cross the gate.

He knows the shape of the operation and the chain of command stopped at a contact he never saw face to face. Manshoon stays invisible.

campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:10: > - **Spoiler limit:** everything above the `## Overview` may be read by the GM only. The Overview is the common-knowledge tier and never mentions Yellowspire, Amath or Manshoon. Neighbors take the tower for a private home.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:38: - **Chosen:** His Chosen become warlords or whispering advisers who tempt rulers to greater cruelty (FRHoF). The wiki names Fzoul Chembryl as his Chosen and the head of his reformed church after 1372 DR (*Forgotten Realms Campaign Setting*, 3e). Chosen in the campaign: none. The campaign history names Fzoul only as the Banite priest who joined Manshoon in 1263 DR.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:62: > - **Waterdeep:** Bane has no public temple, no festival and no city shrine. The one site the campaign ties to him is Yellowspire, and it belongs to Manshoon's cell.

campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:73: > - **Never reveal:** That Yellowspire is a Banite tower, that its cellar circle leads to Kolat Towers, or that Amath is allied with Manshoon. Until the party marks **Manshoon Named**, even the Doom Raiders say "Floxin's cell" or "the other cell" and call Kolat Towers "the towers in the Southern Ward". Before the party reaches the tower, a player who asks about Bane gets the Overview.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:81: > - **Yellowspire and the Zhentarim split:** Amath is allied with Manshoon's cell, the splinter that peeled off the Doom Raiders' network. The Doom Raiders are the other Zhentarim cell and are at war with it. In **The Yellowspire Job** Davil Starsong sends a Doom Raiders member into the tower to steal a ledger of pass-amulet carriers. Davil tells the member that nobody above him has asked for the job and nobody above him will hear of it.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:82: > - **What the tower is for:** Tashlyn Yafeera tells the Doom Raiders member that the Banites keep the place as a temple and a post office. For Manshoon's cell it is also a fallback position, and the cellar circle gives a route into Kolat Towers that skips the pass-amulet requirement. A pressure plate in the circle chamber alerts Kolat Towers. **Faction Outposts** runs the tower as a mini-heist, **Silencing Skeemo** ends at its door, and **Kolat Towers** can be entered through its circle.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:83: > - **Amath Sercent (Lawful Evil, Mulan Human, she/her):** She is a priest of Bane in common clothes, composed and correct. She speaks in courteous, complete sentences, never raises her voice, and treats weakness with controlled contempt. She receives guests as though she were hosting a tea, and the handprints are the only thing in the tower she mentions with warmth. She treats her faith as a settled alignment with Bane, and it predates and outlasts any arrangement with the Zhentarim. If asked, she calls the tower the House of Tyranny and says the neighbors think it a private home, which she prefers.
campaign/setting/deities/bane.md:86: > - **The turning rite:** In the published adventure the five hold a ritual to break the will of a captive broadsheet publisher, Shan Chien, so that he serves Bane. Amath uses it to turn publishers as part of Manshoon's plan to control the flow of information in the city (`sources/adventure-wdh.json:9749`). **The Yellowspire Job** does not use the rite or Shan Chien.

campaign/setting/deities/cyric.md:33: - **Open worship:** Temples to him are forbidden in most nations and thrive in Amn, where his teaching suits a culture of ambition, self-reliance and "buyer beware" (FRHoF; SCAG, 2014). In the early years of his godhood the Moonsea around Zhentil Keep was the center of his worship, and the wiki says the Zhentarim served then as an extension of his church (2e and 3e sources). No campaign file carries that tie into 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/deities/cyric.md:39: - **Temples the wiki names:** Darkhold, the Zhentarim fortress in the Western Heartlands (*Volo's Guide to the Sword Coast*, 5e); the House of Cyric, a subterranean temple beneath Skullport (*Serpent Kingdoms*, 3e); and the Twin Towers of the Eternal Eclipse, the center of his worship in Amn (*Lands of Intrigue: Amn*, 3e). None is in Waterdeep.

campaign/setting/deities/mystra.md:63: > - **The ordinary Waterdhavian's view:** Voice her as the patron of every spellcaster in the city. A sage, a priest or a regular at the bar says she is the Lady of Mysteries, the god of magic and of the Weave that every spell is drawn from, and that wizards, sages and alchemists favor her. They add that her priests look out for children with a spark and find them a teacher, and that the House of Wonder in the Sea Ward is the finest temple to the gods of magic in the world. Older Waterdhavians tell that she was once the mortal Midnight, raised to godhood on the slopes of Mount Waterdeep at the end of the Time of Troubles, and a sage adds that Cyric is blamed for murdering her in the Spellplague. Anyone who follows city politics knows that the Open Lord, Laeral Silverhand, is one of her daughters. Nobody in this conversation mentions Manshoon.

In **Gralhund Villa** the party does not learn his name, his goals or the Weave motive, so no priest of Mystra can speak of it before the party's evidence supports it.

campaign/setting/deities/orcus.md:72: > - **Campaign ties:** No campaign file uses this power. No file ties him to the Cassalanters, the Zhentarim or any other faction. campaign/setting/deities/torm.md:39: - **The Order of the Golden Lion:** This knightly order is based around Tantras, the city with the greatest concentration of Torm worshipers in Faerûn. Its knights each devote themselves to righting one of three Debts. Persecution means aiding good religions, Dereliction means rooting out cults of Bane and routing the Zhentarim, and Destruction means reporting and repairing dead and wild magic (FRHoF). Members wear golden pauldrons carved as lion heads, and a candidate must first complete a holy quest for a temple of Torm. The wiki adds that the pontiff Barriltar Bhandraddon ruled from the Temple of Torm's Coming in Tantras, with no edition tagged. This order is a different body from the Order of the Gauntlet.
campaign/setting/deities/torm.md:70: > - **A paladin PC:** Oath of Devotion. FRHoF says he is a patron god of many paladins and embodies the tenets of that oath, so the fit is sourced and not derived. A paladin who follows the Order of the Golden Lion would take one of its three Debts, and FRHoF lists cults of Bane and the Zhentarim among the targets of the Debt of Dereliction. The Zhentarim are one of the campaign's four villain factions, but no campaign file uses the Golden Lion.

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:71: Several more officers have no Notable Figures page, so they stay as plain text. Hyustus Staget (Lawful Good, Illuskan Human, he/him) is the uptight captain who keeps the peace in the Dock Ward and has been dragging his heels on the Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild, since he reasons that they may as well destroy each other. If he has died, the published adventure replaces him with Cressa Galavarco (Lawful Good, Tethyrian Human, she/her), a veteran. Officers Jonn Bannock (Lawful Neutral, Illuskan Human, he/him) and Ysa Terrow (Lawful Neutral, Tethyrian Human, she/her) work the late shift in **Ten Nights in the City of the Dead**, where they keep the party off the Brandath ground.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:81: > - **Who can be bought:** Corylus Thann, a Masked Lord whom Manshoon's cell blackmails, keeps eight crooked Watch veterans who crack down on anyone who annoys him, and he clears any charge against them. If **Cultists Escaped** is marked in **The Fouled Channel**, the Splinter pays Watch sergeants to ignore reports about the Trades Ward cisterns, and **Vault of Dragons** reads this as reduced Watch cooperation in that ward. In the **Kolat Towers** structure document, Mirt says someone with civic influence has reduced Watch patrol coverage around the towers over eighteen months. Jalester calls the coverage informally diminished and suspects Masked Lord influence that he cannot prove without evidence.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:85: > - **After Gralhund Villa:** The Watch arrives at the front gate as Fel'rekt's team withdraws, and by the morning of Ches 25 it has sealed the villa and begun processing twenty-three bodies. Davil Starsong is arrested two days after the villa and held at Castle Waterdeep, and the Watch cracks down on the Black Network. **Davil's Arrest** and **Davil's Return** carry the consequences, and Tashlyn deals with a constable by showing the badge of a City Guard captain.

campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:33: - The wiki's account of the first holder: a beholder hired by the Zhentarim to start a cell in Skullport. It cut them off, built its own guild, and by 1357 DR the Guild was a thieves' guild working through freelance agents under four Councilors. Each Councilor supervised one domain: mercenaries, bookkeeping, thievery or blackmail (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar's Thieves' Guild; Waterdeep and the North). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:65: - The Guild has rivals below. A drow mage named Rizzeryl works for House Auvryndar and for the Zhentarim, and he and his wererats harry Guild forces on one of the upper levels (WDMM). Which Zhentarim he serves is not settled for the remix, and Draft Notes lists it.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:70: - The war with Manshoon's Zhentarim is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward. It began when Manshoon's envoys came to Skullport three months before the fireball with Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr, and Xanathar killed them.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:80: > - Nihiloor, the mind flayer, has been psionically dominating Xanathar for the last four months. The Xanathar that the party meets, including the one who killed Manshoon's envoys and sent response teams against the party's allies, has been Nihiloor's instrument. Xanathar does not know.

campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:122: > - **The campaign additions to X33.** A secret door from X6 (DC 17 Wisdom (Perception) check) leads to the room. It holds the remains and effects of every previous holder, evidence that the title is claimed through murder, the original charter signed by the first holder, and a locked coffer with the personal journals of the last three Xanathars. The journals record early dealings with Manshoon's predecessors.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:123: > - **What the party can learn.** The room proves that "Xanathar" is a title and that thirteen holders precede the current one. campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:7: > - **What this is:** The Era page for the Zhentarim, from Manshoon's founding of the Black Network to the two rival cells that now share its name in Waterdeep. It is the fourth of eight eras, after **Bregan D'aerthe's Long Road** and before **A Debt in Blood**.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:8: > - **Where it is used:** The GM reads it before voicing Davil Starsong, Tashlyn Yafeera or any Zhentarim NPC, and before answering a contact's question about which Zhentarim the party has met. Every scene with a "Zhentarim" NPC needs a decision about which cell is present.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:10: > - **Spoiler level:** Everything above the Overview is GM-only. The Overview gives the Black Network as most Waterdhavians know it, through the Doom Raiders, and mentions no clone, no second cell and no Kolat Towers.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:12: **Purpose:** Lets the GM say who founded the Black Network, why two cells in Waterdeep use its name, and what each cell believes about the other.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:14: **Keywords:** Zhentarim, Black Network, Manshoon, Zhentil Keep, stasis clones, Manshoon Wars, Doom Raiders, Davil Starsong, Kolat Towers, the Splinter
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:16: **Span:** 1261 DR to 1492 DR. The founding date, 1261 DR, comes from the Forgotten Realms Wiki. The Waterdeep chapter runs from 1489 DR, when the Doom Raiders were sent to found a foothold, to the campaign opening on Ches 1, 1492 DR. Manshoon's cell arrived after them, about two years before the campaign. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:18: **Other Names:** The Black Network; the Splinter (the name the factions use for Manshoon's cell); the Two Zhentarims (a GM name from the guides). "The Founding of Chains" is the old era title, and no in-world name for the era is sourced. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:22: > - Manshoon, a wizard-lord of Zhentil Keep, founded the Zhentarim in 1261 DR as a mercantile network and spread it from the Moonsea to the Sword Coast. The Black Network outlived his death in 1370 DR and lost its capital in the century after.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:23: > - Manshoon survived that death through the stasis clone spell and fought his own clones in the Manshoon Wars. The man in Kolat Towers is a clone of a clone who claims to be the true Manshoon, and he keeps a simulacrum in reserve against assassination.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:24: > - The Doom Raiders, five retired adventurers, were sent to Waterdeep in 1489 DR to found the Black Network's foothold. They regard themselves as the true Zhentarim of Waterdeep.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:25: > - Manshoon's clone arrived later, took over Kolat Towers and built a cell of his own, the Splinter, in about two years. He peeled off a large part of the Doom Raiders' local network.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:26: > - Manshoon's cell regards the Doom Raiders as deserters who stole his name, and the Doom Raiders are at active internal war with it. The two cells will not cooperate. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:28: ### The Black Network
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:30: - Manshoon was born in Zhentil Keep in 1229 DR and became one of the city's lords. He founded the Zhentarim in 1261 DR, and the Banite priest Fzoul Chembryl joined him in 1263 DR.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:31: - The published adventure calls Manshoon "one of the founders" of the Zhentarim (`adventure-wdh.json` lines 560 and 32216), and this page keeps that wording. The wiki calls him the founder.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:32: - Manshoon set his wizards to open trade routes between the Moonsea and the Sword Coast. The Network began as a mercantile concern and grew into a company that trades mercenaries and goods, weapons included.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:33: - Zhentil Keep fell in stages. Its northern half was ruined about 1369 DR after Cyric's fall, and the Shadovar razed the whole city in 1383 DR over the Zhentarim's alliance with the phaerimm.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:34: - The Black Network has an open recruitment policy, and it prizes tenacity and loyalty without requiring either.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:36: - The Zhentarim are now reforming a council, and efforts are underway to reclaim and rebuild Zhentil Keep. The Doom Raiders see the vault's gold as the price of a seat on that council.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:37: - Under the leadership of the Pereghost, the wiki says, the Zhentarim changed from a conquering force into a mercenary army that served others. By 1489 DR they were said to have more members than ever and a reputation for service (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Zhentarim; Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide, 5e). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:42: - Manshoon built the stasis clone spell as insurance against his enemies. Over the years he died several times and returned in a new body.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:43: - In 1370 DR Fzoul and Lord Orgauth killed him and found themselves facing a dozen or more clones. Each clone believed he was the real Manshoon and felt driven to destroy the others. These fights are the Manshoon Wars.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:44: - Three clones survived. One rejoined the Zhentarim and conceded leadership to Fzoul, one became the vampire Orbakh in Westgate, and one took refuge in Undermountain with Halaster.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:46: - Simulacra are a separate tool. Manshoon makes copies of himself with the simulacrum spell, and the copy in Kolat Towers has more hit points and less spellcasting than he does.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:47: - The man in Waterdeep is "a clone of a clone". He claims to be the one true Manshoon, as every surviving clone does.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:48: - The Zhentarim outlasted the 1370 DR death. Fzoul led the Network after it, and one Manshoon clone served as a free agent under him.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:49: - The old outline says the Network "retained only his methods" after Manshoon's coherent leadership ended. No source supports this. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:53: - The Doom Raiders were five adventurers who plundered the lairs of liches, which some call "dooms". They gave up adventuring and joined the reformed Zhentarim in the late 1480s.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:55: - They were sent to Waterdeep in 1489 DR to establish a Zhentarim foothold. The published adventure says only that they came "three years ago".
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:59: - The cell prefers contracts to confrontation and holds that the Black Network works best as a legitimate enterprise. Its agreements hold, and its violence is purposeful.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:60: - Davil wants to end the street violence that Manshoon's cell has caused, because a legitimate criminal enterprise cannot run in a city where an illegitimate one keeps shooting people.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:61: - The Doom Raiders operate openly, meet in taverns and recruit adventurers. They are the first Zhentarim contact the party meets, during **Trollskull Alley**.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:62: - The Doom Raiders believe they are the true Zhentarim of Waterdeep. The old outline says Davil's cell "separates from Manshoon on pragmatic grounds". The sources reverse that direction: the cell came first, and Manshoon's clone peeled off its network.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:64: ### Manshoon's Waterdeep Operation
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:68: - Manshoon's clone fled Undermountain, found the towers abandoned with the force field intact, and took the amulet-making instructions from Duhlark's study. He spent two years building the Splinter.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:71: - The clone arrived after the Doom Raiders and peeled off a large part of the local network they had built. Through it he learned that Neverember had used the local Zhentarim's resources in the embezzlement.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:73: - The published adventure gives him a different goal: to oust the Open Lord, replace the City Watch with Black Network mercenaries, and then turn on Halaster. The campaign keeps the Weave goal, which is set out on the **Manshoon** villain page.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:74: - Manshoon's supporters among the Zhentarim want Xanathar destroyed and the city's economy in their hands. The Doom Raiders want to expose Manshoon before the authorities drive them out.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:75: - The old outline says the towers were set up out of sight and that Manshoon "read Neverember's ouster as political opportunity". The nearest source for the second claim is the Alexandrian remix, which has him learn of the embezzlement through the Doom Raiders' network. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:79: - Two cells use the name Zhentarim in Waterdeep, and each denies the other's claim. The distinction should become clear to attentive players during **Trollskull Alley**, because the Doom Raiders' professionalism makes Manshoon's operatives land harder when they appear.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:80: - The Doom Raiders call themselves the legitimate Black Network in the city. They are at active internal war with Manshoon's cell, and anyone who joins them is caught in the crossfire.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:81: - Manshoon's cell does not acknowledge the Doom Raiders as Zhentarim. It regards them as deserters who stole his name. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:82: - The cells will not cooperate or share intelligence, and where their operations overlap they undermine each other. Information given to the Doom Raiders does not reach Manshoon's agents.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:83: - The Doom Raiders have a leak of their own. Skeemo Weirdbottle has been feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell since before the party was recruited.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:84: - Factions that speak to players say "the Splinter" or "the other cell" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the listener.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:88: The Zhentarim, also called the Black Network, is a mercenary and trading company founded in Zhentil Keep in 1261 DR, a city that has since fallen to ruin. In Waterdeep it is known mainly through the Doom Raiders, a band of retired adventurers who came to the city in 1489 DR and describe themselves as the Zhentarim of Waterdeep. They sell mercenary work and lend money through their businesses, and they welcome adventurers who are looking for contracts. The Black Network has long wanted a place in the city's politics, and the strength of the Masked Lords, the nobility and the guilds has kept it out.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:92: > - Waterdeep holds two Zhentarim organizations, and the Doom Raiders are only one of them. Nobody speaks Manshoon's name to a player until **Manshoon Named** is marked for that listener.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:93: > - The Harpers do not know that the Splinter is Manshoon's cell or that it answers to Kolat Towers. No Harper contact names him.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:94: > - The man in Kolat Towers is a clone who believes he is the true Manshoon, and he keeps a simulacrum in reserve. The old outline asked whether he was the original or a simulacrum, and he is a third thing.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:98: **Tone:** Measured and factual, in the Ember history register. The Doom Raiders are sincere and Manshoon is patient. State each cell's view of the other as that cell's own belief, and give no narrator ruling on which one is the true Zhentarim.
campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:105: - `campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md`: the Two Zhentarims note, the relationship to the Doom Raiders, and Manshoon's goals.

campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:112: - `sources/1. The Villains.pdf`, Alexandrian Part 1, p.16: the peel-off and Neverember's use of Zhentarim resources.

campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:114: - Forgotten Realms Wiki, Zhentarim (edition cited: Ruins of Zhentil Keep; Forgotten Realms Campaign Setting 3rd edition; Forgotten Realms Campaign Guide 4e; Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide 5e): founding, Fzoul, Banedeath, the 1370 DR death, the 1383 DR razing, the Pereghost and the 1489 DR headcount claim.

campaign/setting/history/04-the-founding-of-chains.md:151: > - **Conflicts:** Clone survivors. The body keeps the 3e account that three clones survived 1370 DR. The wiki's later account is that the 1383 DR razing killed the Zhentarim clone and Fzoul, the Undermountain clone died in the Spellplague, and only the vampire Orbakh remained (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Zhentarim and Manshoon; Forgotten Realms Campaign Guide, 4e). The published adventure wins: its Waterdeep clone is "long thought dead" (`adventure-wdh.json` line 472), which is the Undermountain clone, and the man in Kolat Towers is that clone. "Three survived" describes the state after 1370 DR only.

campaign/setting/history/08-the-players-arrive.md:73: Waterdeep enters the spring of 1492 DR as a prosperous city with a restless underworld. Brawls between the Xanathar's Guild and the Zhentarim have become common in the Dock Ward, and the City Watch is called to them often. Several noble households have taken delivery of mechanical servants bought from a foreign dealer, and the city is preparing for Fleetswake, the maritime festival that fills the harbor at the end of Ches.

campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:40: - **Eye #1, Xanathar:** Manshoon's Zhentarim stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. About three months before the fireball, Manshoon sent two lieutenants to Skullport with it as a gift. Xanathar ate them and kept the Eye, and Ott Steeltoes put it in the bowl of Sylgar, Xanathar's goldfish, because it was a pretty color. Xanathar does not know what the Eye is.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:42: - **Eye #3, Jarlaxle:** It began in Renaer's locket. Manshoon's cell seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse. Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried the Eye off with the rest of the back office, and nobody in the Guild recognized it. Bregan D'aerthe agents posing as guards lifted it from a strongbox in the hideout before dawn, and it reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:44: - **Manshoon:** He holds no Eye. He needs the Stone and all three Eyes, and his plan is to let someone else open the vault and then take it from them.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:95: - **What the party can learn early:** **Vault Opening Method Known** records that the party has the Melairkyn notes (dragon scale, mithral hammer and sunlight). **Eyes Letter Found** records the unsigned letter that confirms the three Eyes. Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game in **Kolat Towers** (E9) carries a partly decoded command phrase, and finishing it needs the Full Awakening or more research.

campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:57: - **The sunlight:** The *daylight* spell suffices, and the structure doc rules that its light counts as sunlight under the 2024 rules. Manshoon's sunglobe also works if the party marked **Manshoon Destroyed** and recovered the item at **Kolat Towers**. Otherwise twenty large steel mirrors (400 gp in all) and several hours of alignment will bring sunlight down.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:59: - **Learning the ceremony:** Four independent paths lead to it. The Stone's vision during the Full Awakening gives its shape, in the lines "Three keys ye seek. Strike scale of wyrm in light of day. As the vault bears witness, so shall it ope to mithral's strike." Kalain's notes in **Kalain's Tower** give it in full, in Neverember's hand. The Cassalanters know it from their own investigation and share it if the party's standing with them allows. Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game from **Kolat Towers** (area E9) gives an outline and a partial Elvish command phrase.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:95: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim:** They know the Stone exists and unlocks great wealth.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:164: > - **Conflicts:** How the vault opens. `running-the-villains.md:32` says it opens only "when someone presents the complete Stone of Golorr, with all three Eyes restored, and speaks the command phrase the Stone reveals". The campaign ceremony is a dragon scale, sunlight and a mithral hammer (`arc-j:113-119`, `fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:72`). The Stone's vision gives the ceremony's shape (`arc-j:123`), and the command phrase appears in Manshoon's Report (`arc-j:126`) and is revealed in full by the Stone's Full Awakening. The page follows the ceremony.
