campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:13: **Emotions.** Jelenn most often feels desperate anxiety about debt, wounded pride, bitter regret over her failed investments, suspicious hostility toward strangers, fraying dignity, and private terror of Manshoon's leverage.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:15: **Motivations.** Jelenn wants to escape Manshoon's blackmail, repay her debts without any other noble learning of them, and reclaim the financial independence that her bad investments destroyed.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Jelenn is a proud noblewoman in her fifties who travels with sixteen guards and has no time for strangers. To those she trusts, she is a Masked Lord who borrowed from the Zhentarim because she feared gossip more than gangsters. Deep down, she knows the decision was catastrophic, and Manshoon's blackmail is the price of her pride.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:23: **Morale.** In a confrontation, Jelenn would demand the party leave and threaten dire consequences. Her threat is mostly bluster. If freed from Manshoon, she grants a Special Favor. If blackmailed by the party instead, she pretends to cooperate while secretly working against them.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/04-jelenn-urmbrusk.md:25: **Relationships.** Jelenn is a Masked Lord of Waterdeep, a debtor of the Zhentarim (borrowed to avoid borrowing from rival nobles), and a blackmail victim of Manshoon's cell whose name appears on the Kolat Towers blackmail list.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:15: **Motivations.** Davil wants to keep the Doom Raiders operational and independent from Manshoon's splinter, maintain peace with Xanathar's Guild through negotiation rather than violence, and protect the people who chose to follow him.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Davil is the Master of Opportunities and Negotiations: a sun elf who makes friends easily, negotiates deals with grace even while drunk, speaks with a lilt he attributes to his elven heritage, and wears a charm bracelet hung with small trophies from monsters the Doom Raiders have killed. He conducts business from a curtained room on the Yawning Portal's second floor, with a permanent suite on the third. To those he trusts, he is a retired adventurer who joined the Zhentarim with his companions and frames the whole enterprise as helping people in need. Deep down, he withholds explanation and lets things sit, showing interest without revealing why, and he was fond of Skeemo in a way he does not say aloud.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:25: **Relationships.** Davil is the cell leader of the Doom Raiders (Waterdeep Zhentarim), the faction contact for Zhentarim-aligned PCs, a permanent resident at the Yawning Portal (second floor business room, third floor suite), a colleague of Istrid, Tashlyn, Ziraj, and the traitor Skeemo Weirdbottle, and the unknowing target of Nihiloor's intellect devourer, with Meloon watching him and waiting for a clean opportunity to kill him without witnesses.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/02-yagra-stonefist.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/02-yagra-stonefist.md:15: **Motivations.** Yagra wants to protect Davil Starsong, keep the Doom Raiders safe from Manshoon's splinter, and arm-wrestle anyone who looks like they might be interesting.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/02-yagra-stonefist.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Yagra is seven feet of purplish-grey half-orc wearing a red jade Zhentarim pendant openly, cracking her knuckles at the Yawning Portal bar. To those she trusts, she arm-wrestles patrons because people relax after losing to her. Deep down, she tells the story of the slaver ship she was on before the Doom Raiders found her like an old weather report, wanting nothing for the telling.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/02-yagra-stonefist.md:29: A towering purplish-grey half-orc wearing a Zhentarim pendant openly at the Yawning Portal bar, who arm-wrestles patrons as her preferred method of introduction and has never been seen to lose.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/03-istrid-horn.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/03-istrid-horn.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Istrid is the Black Network's Master of Trade and Coin, running a lending operation from a guarded Dock Ward warehouse at rates comparable to the Cassalanters'. To those she trusts, she is the Doom Raiders' financial backbone. Deep down, she likes having people indebted to her, and the difference between her interest rates and a legitimate lender's rates is the silence when you miss a payment.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/03-istrid-horn.md:25: **Relationships.** Istrid is the Doom Raiders' Master of Trade and Coin, Emmek Frewn's creditor (150 gp loan with Zhentarim enforcement), and a worshipper of Vergadain whose lending operation funds the cell's operations.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/04-skeemo-weirdbottle.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/04-skeemo-weirdbottle.md:15: **Motivations.** Skeemo wants to maintain access to rare alchemical components (which Manshoon provides), avoid detection as a traitor for as long as possible, and survive no matter which side wins.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/04-skeemo-weirdbottle.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Skeemo is the Doom Raiders' Master of Magic, running Weirdbottle's Concoctions in the Trades Ward. To those he trusts, he is small, precise, and pleasant in a way that has no warmth in it. Deep down, he has been a traitor since before the party was recruited, bought by Manshoon with rare components and Watch-inspection protection, messaging both sides by paper bird.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/04-skeemo-weirdbottle.md:25: **Relationships.** Skeemo is the Doom Raiders' Master of Magic, a traitor feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell and House Gralhund, and a colleague Davil was fond of, which Davil does not say aloud.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/05-tashlyn-yafeera.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/06-ziraj-the-hunter.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/07-senna-vael.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/force-grey/01-vajra-safahr-the-blackstaff.md:25: **Relationships.** Vajra is the Blackstaff of Waterdeep, the commander of Force Grey, the wielder of a staff containing Khelben Arunsun's soul, and a strained subordinate of Laeral Silverhand. She has rare, dry humor, which she delivers flat: "The Splinter tried to kill me through a junior arcanist. That's either an insult or a compliment. I haven't decided." She says "the Splinter" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for the party member she is speaking to, and only then says his name.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/01-yalah-gralhund.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** House Gralhund (Zhentarim-aligned)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/01-yalah-gralhund.md:15: **Motivations.** Yalah wants to become a Masked Lord through bribery, blackmail, or extortion, secure House Gralhund's prosperity for generations, and maintain control of the Zhentarim resources housed at her villa without Manshoon discovering she considers Urstul an underling.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/01-yalah-gralhund.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Yalah is the lady of House Gralhund, whose motto is "We see both sides." She has a keen mind and the wisdom to discern friend from foe. To those she trusts, she commands the Black Network resources at her villa and treats Urstul Floxin as her employee. Deep down, she pried the Stone from Floxin at sword-point and holds it herself — she does not trust a construct with this much leverage. Previous bids to become a Masked Lord were thwarted, and the gold in the Vault represents her last best chance.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/01-yalah-gralhund.md:23: **Morale.** In a confrontation, Yalah would feign innocence, redirect blame, and rely on Hrabbaz and her Zhentarim retainers. She does not fight unless cornered, and even then she negotiates.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/01-yalah-gralhund.md:25: **Relationships.** Yalah is the wife of Orond Gralhund (whom she manages more than loves), the commander of Hrabbaz (her bodyguard, more loyal to her than to Orond), and the de facto handler of the Zhentarim forces at her villa, not realizing their true master is Manshoon.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/02-orond-gralhund.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Orond is the patriarch of House Gralhund, a short, stocky man who dresses well and is easy on the eyes. To those who deal with him, his boorish nature, inflated ego, and despicable opinions about "the common rabble" emerge whenever Yalah is not present to quiet him. Deep down, he was born with a tiefling's tail (amputated as a boy; the scar remains), does not understand how entangled his family has become with the Black Network, and keeps Hrabbaz close for fear that others might attack and rob him at any moment.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/03-hrabbaz.md:25: **Relationships.** Hrabbaz is the bodyguard of both Gralhunds (shared, though more loyal to Yalah), and a half-orc whose impeccable manners and wardrobe make him the most unsettling presence in a villa full of Zhentarim.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/05-chirada.md:15: **Motivations.** Chirada wants to recover the Eye of the Stone of Golorr she believes the Zhentarim took, for what remains of Gralhund House interests, track the movements of whoever holds the Stone, and complete the mission described in the Missive from Castle Ward without becoming another casualty of the Grand Game.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/01-dagult-neverember.md:7: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Vault of Dragons**, **The Stone's Other Master**, **Gralhund Villa (location)**, **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Cassalanter Villa (location)**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/02-renaer-neverember.md:7: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Vault of Dragons**, **The Stone's Other Master**, **The Dragon in the Harbor**, **Gralhund Villa (location)**, **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Xanathar Sewer Hideout**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/04-floon-blagmaar.md:7: > - **Featured in:** **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Zhentarim Warehouse**, **Xanathar Sewer Hideout**, **Trollskull Manor**
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:15: **Motivations.** Dalakhar wanted to deliver the Stone of Golorr to the party (planning to reclaim it later for Neverember), avoid the Zhentarim and Xanathar agents closing in, and serve the man he believed was Waterdeep's rightful Open Lord.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Dalakhar was a small gnome with dry waste on his boots and sewer-stink on his cloak. To those he trusted, he was Neverember's most reliable agent, the one who succeeded where many others had failed. Deep down, he was afraid that the Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild were close to catching him, and he was right.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/18-lady-gondafrey.md:25: **Relationships.** Lady Gondafrey is a City Watch captain from 1379 DR who became a prisoner of Duhlark Kolat and subsequently of Manshoon, a potential **Kolat Towers** ally whose alignment is unstable on a daily basis, and a witness to decades of Kolat Towers' history.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:1: # Manshoon
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:7: > - **Villain profile:** see **Manshoon** in Villains
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:12: **Resonance.** Manshoon should inspire intellectual dread with his patient precision, frustrated rage from the party's inability to pin him down, and dark fascination with a centuries-old wizard who lost an arm to Halaster Blackcloak and refuses to stop scheming.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:14: **Emotions.** Manshoon most often feels cold patient calculation, contemptuous superiority, precise anger reserved for specific targets, possessive ambition, frustrated pride about his artificial arm, and the absolute certainty that he is the one true Manshoon.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:16: **Motivations.** Manshoon wants to acquire the Stone of Golorr for the ancient Weave knowledge encoded in its aboleth memory, use that knowledge to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra, fund his preparations with the vault's gold, and deepen his hold over Masked Lords Corylus Thann and Jelenn Urmbrusk into a full Council majority.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:18: **Inspirations.** When playing Manshoon, channel Magneto (*X-Men*), Emperor Palpatine (*Star Wars*), and Moriarty (*Sherlock Holmes*).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Manshoon does not exist; he is never seen entering or leaving Kolat Towers, using a teleportation circle for all movement. To those he trusts, he is a surviving clone of one of the Zhentarim's founders who fled Undermountain after Halaster amputated his left arm, crafted an artificial replacement, and now orchestrates a bid for total control of Waterdeep. Deep down, he claims to be the one true Manshoon, as all surviving clones do. He creates simulacra of himself and takes great pains to conceal his identity.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:24: **Morale.** In combat, Manshoon lets his simulacrum absorb the opening volley while he casts from behind it. He prefers to capture rather than kill (prisoners answer questions; corpses do not), and this habit creates tactical openings the party can exploit. Only those who breach his extradimensional sanctum see his full power.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:26: **Relationships.** Manshoon is the leader of the Zhentarim splinter cell, the employer of Urstul Floxin, Sidra Romeir, and Vevette Blackwater, a blackmailer of Masked Lords Jelenn Urmbrusk and Corylus Thann, and a clone-wizard whose response teams are deliberately the weakest in muscle because drawing attention is the one mistake he will not make twice.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/01-manshoon.md:30: A name the party does not have yet. The hard-edged Zhentarim operatives working Waterdeep's streets answer to someone, and none of them will say who.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:15: **Motivations.** Urstul wants to recover the Stone of Golorr for Manshoon, maintain operational control of the field team, and survive encounters with the party by any means necessary, including hostage-taking.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Urstul is a large, heavyset man in his forties who treats every room as his own. To those he works with, he is Manshoon's chief field operative who runs both the Dalakhar capture attempt and the Gralhund Villa operation. Deep down, he will not divulge his master's name unless magically compelled, and he keeps a collection of black flying snakes as couriers because he trusts animals more than people.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:25: **Relationships.** Urstul is Manshoon's chief field operative, a temporary occupant of Gralhund Villa (where he treats Yalah as an underling), and the antagonist who drives **Fireball!** and **Gralhund Villa** before the party can identify his master.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/02-urstul-floxin.md:29: A large, heavyset man in his forties who carries himself as though every room belongs to him. He works for the Zhentarim and makes no effort to hide it, though he will not name who gives him orders.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:15: **Motivations.** Vevette wants to serve Manshoon's interests while positioning herself for advancement, keep the Stone in Zhentarim hands, and avoid imprisonment by exploiting the law.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/03-vevette-blackwater.md:25: **Relationships.** Vevette is Manshoon's most trusted field lieutenant, the coordinator of strike team deployments from Kolat Towers, and a swashbuckler whose operational relationship with the Cassalanters consists of a rebuffed contact and a kept visiting card.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:11: **Resonance.** Sidra should inspire wariness with her unhurried calm (she has seen worse than the party and it shows), respect for the way she runs Kolat Towers like a barracks, and frustration with a loyalty to Manshoon that no argument will crack.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:15: **Motivations.** Sidra wants to maintain Kolat Towers' security, protect the teleport ring system, and ensure that Manshoon's operations run without interruption.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Sidra is a Calishite veteran who assumes intruding PCs are guests of Manshoon, since Kolat Towers' force field screens out riffraff. She offers water, asks their business, and watches their hands. To those she trusts, she holds the teleport rings when lieutenants leave the Towers and hands them to replacements without ceremony when the previous holder does not come back. Deep down, she has outlasted every other lieutenant Manshoon has employed. She does not wonder why.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/04-sidra-romeir.md:25: **Relationships.** Sidra is Manshoon's Kolat Towers lieutenant, the keeper of the teleporter ring system, and the only person in the cell whose loyalty Manshoon has never tested, because testing it would mean he does not already know the answer.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/05-agorn-fuoco.md:5: > - *Turami human bard (Manshoon lieutenant), neutral evil, he/him*. Stat block: **Performer**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/05-agorn-fuoco.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/05-agorn-fuoco.md:15: **Motivations.** Agorn wants to avoid consequences for his Zhentarim work, use whatever he knows (Amath's identity, his pass-amulet, Manshoon's operational structure) to buy his freedom, and eventually retire somewhere quieter.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/05-agorn-fuoco.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Agorn Fuoco is a bard who attends the theater, keeps excellent company, and does occasional work for the Zhentarim that he does not ask about too carefully. To those who capture him, he offers his pass-amulet, Amath Sercent's address, Manshoon's general operational structure, and whatever else occurs to him — starting immediately and working outward from least to most useful, in the order most likely to buy another minute. Deep down, he would rather be playing music.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/05-agorn-fuoco.md:25: **Relationships.** Agorn is a Manshoon lieutenant and bard, the partner of Amath Sercent, whom he visits at Yellowspire in the evenings, a pass-amulet holder for Kolat Towers, and a man whose mother lives in Mistshore and knows nothing about any of this.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/06-avareen-windrivver.md:5: > - *Illuskan human spy (Zhentarim interrogator), lawful evil, she/her*. Stat block: **Spy**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/06-avareen-windrivver.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/06-avareen-windrivver.md:15: **Motivations.** Avareen wants to run the Brindul Alley operation to Manshoon's specifications, maintain the pass-amulet's security, and deliver outcomes with minimum exposure.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/06-avareen-windrivver.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Avareen lives in a windowless house in Brindul Alley and is not there to meet her neighbors. To those who work with her, she is reliable, thorough, and personally uninvested in outcomes. Deep down, she has a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers because Manshoon has never had a reason to question her, and she has taken care that no reason develops.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/06-avareen-windrivver.md:25: **Relationships.** Avareen is the Zhentarim interrogation operative at the Brindul Alley Interrogation House, Zorbog Jyarkoth's operational partner, a pass-amulet holder for Kolat Towers, and Ott Steeltoes's current jailer.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/07-zorbog-jyarkoth.md:5: > - *Turami human thug (Zhentarim), neutral evil, he/him*. Stat block: **Thug**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/07-zorbog-jyarkoth.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/08-amath-sercent.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/08-amath-sercent.md:15: **Motivations.** Amath wants to maintain Yellowspire as a Banite cell in Waterdeep, support Manshoon's operations where they serve Bane's interests, and convert, use, or remove people in the order that serves the hierarchy.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/08-amath-sercent.md:25: **Relationships.** Amath is the Banite priestess occupying Yellowspire (Castle Ward), the keeper of a teleportation circle providing a back-route into Kolat Towers (bypassing the pass-amulet requirement), Agorn Fuoco's romantic partner, and a Manshoon-adjacent religious operative whose devotion to Bane predates and outlasts any particular Zhentarim arrangement.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/09-vira-solkan.md:5: > - *Damaran human mage (Manshoon plant), neutral evil, she/her*. Stat block: **Mage Apprentice**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/09-vira-solkan.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/09-vira-solkan.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Vira Solkan is a quiet junior arcanist in her first posting at Blackstaff Tower who does her work, stays late, and does not talk much at meals. To those who investigate her, she has no suspicious contacts, no unusual expenses, and no detectable magical connection to the Splinter's cell. Characters say "the Splinter" until **Manshoon Named** is marked for them, and only then connect her to Manshoon or to Kolat Towers (**Smoke in the Tower**). Deep down, the resonance disruptor is already in place, and she is counting days.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/09-vira-solkan.md:25: **Relationships.** Vira is a Manshoon plant embedded inside Blackstaff Tower, the operative responsible for installing the resonance disruptor in the Tower's defensive architecture, and the primary antagonist of **Smoke in the Tower**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:13: **Emotions.** Manafret most often feels genuine domestic pride, the uncomplicated satisfaction of a well-run kitchen, pleasant curiosity about visitors who have not introduced themselves, loyal warmth toward Manshoon expressed through excellent food, and mild concern when guests seem confused.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:15: **Motivations.** Manafret wants to cook well, keep the kitchen orderly, be useful to Manshoon, and welcome whoever the teleporter ring brings through the sanctum.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Manafret is the Kolat Towers cook who sets an extra place at the table for visitors who may arrive unexpectedly from the sanctum. To those who enter the kitchen through the wrong door, he asks whether they have eaten, what they would prefer, and whether he should let Lord Manshoon know they have arrived. Deep down, he wears the teleporter ring because Manshoon trusts him, and the intruders always seem to respond well to the food.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:23: **Morale.** In a fight, Manafret retreats to a defensible position, calls for help through whatever means are available, and uses his halfling size to make himself a difficult target. He does not use the teleporter ring except on Manshoon's orders.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/10-manafret-cherryport.md:25: **Relationships.** Manafret is Manshoon's Kolat Towers cook and a teleporter ring holder, a lieutenant whose threat is contextual (his ring bypasses the pass-amulet system), and a man whose hospitality toward intruders has probably prevented more deaths than his employer would prefer.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:15: **Motivations.** Kaevja wants to earn her continued place in Manshoon's organization, protect the sanctum against unauthorized entry, and cast the sending that accelerates reinforcements before she is incapacitated.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:21: **Persona.** To the world — specifically to anyone who has breached E2 and triggered the Mask discharge — Kaevja is the mage at E3, already reaching for her component pouch. To Manshoon, she is the sanctum's most reliable early-warning mechanism, valued more for the sending she can cast than for the spells she throws in combat. Deep down, she serves Manshoon because he is the most capable operator she has encountered, and working for the most capable operator available is, in her estimation, the rational choice until demonstrated otherwise.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:23: **Morale.** Kaevja's first action is always the sending — to Manshoon's current location and to E8 — not combat. If she sends successfully and the party's attention is fully on her, she uses the earth elemental gem and fights behind it. If the party eliminates her silently before she can act, she contributes nothing; if she succeeds, she has made the entire operation significantly harder.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/11-kaevja-cynavern.md:25: **Relationships.** Kaevja is Sidra Romeir's sanctum counterpart in E3, Manshoon's primary early-warning mechanism, a mage whose communications role matters more than her combat capability, and the party's most time-sensitive priority target in the extradimensional sanctum.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/12-havia-quickknife.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/12-havia-quickknife.md:11: **Resonance.** Havia should inspire initial underestimation — she is a halfling, she is small, she is in a formal audience chamber — and then immediate correction, because she is in E10 of Manshoon's sanctum and she has been waiting for someone to enter it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/12-havia-quickknife.md:13: **Emotions.** Havia most often feels settled conviction, physical readiness she has to consciously contain, absolute certainty in her service to Manshoon, controlled anticipation in combat, and no ambivalence of any kind about the work.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/12-havia-quickknife.md:15: **Motivations.** Havia wants to serve Manshoon completely and to neutralize any threat that enters the audience chamber. She does not want other things.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/12-havia-quickknife.md:25: **Relationships.** Havia is paired with Mookie Plush as one of two halfling adepts serving as Manshoon's household fanatics in E10, one of the sanctum's most physically capable non-spellcasting combatants, and a source of no useful information if somehow captured — she does not negotiate and knows nothing that would benefit the party.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/13-mookie-plush.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/13-mookie-plush.md:13: **Emotions.** Mookie most often feels cheerful certainty, a kind of undiscriminating goodwill toward everyone in the room regardless of what he is about to do to them, absolute devotion to Manshoon, and genuine puzzlement when opponents expect the warmth to mean something.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/13-mookie-plush.md:15: **Motivations.** Mookie wants to serve Manshoon completely. His warmth is not a tactic; it is simply who he is. These two things coexist without contradiction, from his perspective.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/13-mookie-plush.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Mookie Plush is the friendlier of the two halflings in the audience chamber — he smiles easily, seems genuinely interested in who the party is, and gives a distinct impression that he would rather this did not have to happen. To anyone who probes this affect, it does not crack because there is nothing beneath it to crack: he is genuinely warm, genuinely loyal, and genuinely willing to kill everyone in the room. Deep down, he found what he was looking for when Manshoon offered him a place, and the finding resolved him completely.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:13: **Emotions.** Yorn most often feels steady patience, a physical readiness he carries the way other people carry stillness, satisfaction in his particular utility to Manshoon's operation, and a focused clarity that descends once he understands a situation is no longer going to be avoided.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:15: **Motivations.** Yorn wants to do the work Manshoon brought him in for — enforcement, extraction, the maintenance of internal fear as much as external threat — and to do it well enough that his value is never questioned.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:21: **Persona.** To the world — specifically to anyone brought into the sanctum who is about to be questioned — Yorn the Terror is the fact of what is going to happen. He does not monologue. He does not explain himself. To Manshoon's lieutenants, he is the asset deployed when a situation has moved past the point at which conversation is the primary tool. Deep down, he has been called "the Terror" long enough that he has stopped thinking of it as a name and started thinking of it as a description.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/manshoons-zhentarim/14-yorn-the-terror.md:25: **Relationships.** Yorn is a Manshoon Splinter enforcer stationed in the extradimensional sanctum whose role in the organization is the maintenance of internal fear as much as the exercise of direct violence, and a threat that compounds significantly if encountered late in the operation when the reinforcement timer has already thinned the party's resources.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/trollskull-community/04-fala-lefaliir.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Fala is an outgoing wood elf herbalist with long braided hair who speaks without small talk and has strong opinions about tea. To those they trust, Fala is the person who shelters a Zhentarim assassin because he once saved their life, no questions asked. Deep down, Fala's curiosity runs deeper than their caution, and they know it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/trollskull-community/10-emmek-frewn.md:25: **Relationships.** Emmek is a debtor of Istrid Horn (150 gp loan with interest, enforced by Zhentarim thugs), a client of the Shard Shunners (halfling wererat gang he pays to sabotage the party), and a reluctant neighbor of Tally Fellbranch (who thinks Emmek's pub sign is "an insult to joinery").
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/01-xanathar.md:16: **Motivations.** Xanathar wants to eliminate every Zhentarim operative in Waterdeep, recover the Stone of Golorr, and ensure that no one takes anything that belongs to it, especially Sylgar.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/06-ott-steeltoes.md:25: **Relationships.** Ott is Xanathar's fishkeeper (all Sylgar replacements have passed through him), a Zuggtmoy worshipper who cultivates mushrooms in his quarters, a Zhentarim prisoner at the Interrogation House, a field agent of Xanathar's Guild, and a detailed intelligence source for anyone who rescues him and asks the right questions about the Lair. He is also the one who put Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr in Sylgar's bowl as a pretty stone, and he has no idea what it is.
campaign/setting/organizations/01-harpers.md:5: > - **Campaign role:** Player faction — intelligence-gathering operatives working against tyranny and the consolidation of the Black Network's ruthless half.
campaign/setting/organizations/01-harpers.md:16: In Waterdeep, the Harpers maintain strong ties to the city's intellectual and artistic communities. Their primary concern in the current moment is the rivalry inside the Black Network and the ruthless newcomers they call the Splinter, which seems to act on Harper information. They are also quietly alert to the Stone of Golorr — they believe it contains intelligence of strategic importance and want it in their hands.
campaign/setting/organizations/02-lords-alliance.md:39: > - Say "the Splinter" in front of players. Use Manshoon's name only for a member for whom **Manshoon Named** is marked.
campaign/setting/organizations/03-emerald-enclave.md:40: > - Phaulkonmere stands one block south of Kolat Towers. Until **Manshoon Named** is marked for a member, Enclave speakers say "the Splinter" and never point toward the towers.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:1: # The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:5: > - **Campaign role:** Player faction — the legitimate Black Network cell in Waterdeep, here since 1489 DR and at active war with Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:10: > *Contact: Davil Starsong, always at the Yawning Portal. He keeps a room upstairs and does all his business in the taproom, which he has decided is safer than a private office and more honest than a warehouse. He's a sun elf who looks like a man who has decided that charm is both a genuine quality and an excellent professional tool, and has spent enough decades cultivating the combination that he can no longer tell where one ends and the other begins. He uses an elven lute as a spellcasting focus and has it slung across his back during every meeting. He negotiates with grace and aplomb even when he's been drinking, which is most of the time. He is genuinely invested in ending the street violence Manshoon's cell has caused. His investment in ending the violence is the position of someone who cannot run a legitimate criminal enterprise in a city where an illegitimate one keeps shooting people.*
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:14: > *After Mission 2, the City Watch arrests Davil on suspicion of involvement in the Gralhund Villa incident. Contact passes to **Tashlyn Yafeera**, who communicates exclusively by flying snake. She is Master of Arms and Mercenaries and serves as Captain of the City Guard at the South Gate under Vorondar Levelstone, a dwarf magister whose trust she has carefully built over two years. She is precise and carries sharper intelligence than Davil, because she has been spending the past year building sources inside Manshoon's cell rather than business contacts in the Trades Ward. Her briefings use fewer words and expect more inference.*
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:20: *"The best thing about being part of the Black Network? The Network is always there when you need it."*
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:22: The Doom Raiders are a cell of experienced Zhentarim operators who arrived in Waterdeep in 1489 DR, the first Zhentarim cell in the city, and built a network of commercial fronts they genuinely believe in. They are professional criminals who prefer contracts to confrontations and sincerely believe the Black Network works best when it operates like a legitimate enterprise. Their agreements hold. Their violence is purposeful, never gratuitous.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:24: The Doom Raiders and Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter cell are at active war. Manshoon's cell came later and peeled off the Doom Raiders' network, and the two cells despise each other: information shared with the Doom Raiders will not reach Manshoon's agents. However, characters who join the Doom Raiders are joining the older cell and will inevitably be caught in the crossfire as Manshoon moves to eliminate Davil's cell entirely. This is a structural feature of the campaign.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:26: **Primary Contact:** Davil Starsong — a sun elf retired adventurer who leads the Doom Raiders' Waterdeep operation. Charming, direct, and genuinely invested in ending the street violence Manshoon's cell has caused. Tashlyn Yafeera handles the cell's business whenever Davil is unavailable.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:28: **Secondary Contact:** Tashlyn Yafeera — takes over mission briefings after Davil's arrest, communicating by flying snake. She has sharper intelligence on Manshoon's faction than Davil and her assignments reflect it.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:37: - **Skeemo Weirdbottle** — Master of Magic; runs Weirdbottle's Concoctions in the Trades Ward. Traitor feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell. See the **Skeemo Weirdbottle** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:44: Davil's cell sees an enormous opportunity in the half-million dragons — efforts are underway to reclaim and rebuild Zhentil Keep, and that money could buy the Doom Raiders a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council. PCs who recover the gold and hand it to the Doom Raiders are effectively buying their patrons into continental leadership. This is a real choice with real consequences.
campaign/setting/organizations/06-doom-raiders.md:52: The Doom Raiders are a cell of experienced Zhentarim operators running commercial fronts in Waterdeep — professional criminals who prefer contracts to confrontations. They are at active war with a rival Zhentarim cell that peeled off their network, which makes every piece of intelligence the party brings them valuable and every mission a potential move in a larger fight.
campaign/setting/organizations/08-xanathars-guild.md:25: - **Ott Steeltoes** — Dwarf cult fanatic (Zuggtmoy), neutral evil. Guild field agent, currently held prisoner in the Zhentarim Interrogation House. Wears a leather skullcap stitched with fake beholder eyestalks. If rescued and cultivated, he can lead the PCs toward Xanathar's Lair. See the **Ott Steeltoes** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:1: # Manshoon's Zhentarim
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:7: > - **Villain profile:** see **Manshoon** in Villains
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:12: **A note on the Zhentarim:** Two cells operate in Waterdeep. Davil Starsong's **Doom Raiders** are the player-joinable faction covered on **The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)** organization page. Manshoon's splinter is a completely separate organization that peeled off from the Doom Raiders' local network when he returned to Waterdeep. The two groups dislike each other. When players ally with the Zhentarim through Davil, they're working with the Doom Raiders, not Manshoon. The villains in this section answer only to Manshoon.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:14: Manshoon is a centuries-old wizard, a clone of a clone of the original Black Network's founder, who claims, as all surviving Manshoon clones do, to be the one true Manshoon. The gold means nothing to him as wealth. He wants the Stone of Golorr for what it knows: the Stone is a transformed aboleth, and encoded in its centuries of absorbed memory is ancient knowledge of the Weave's structure. Manshoon intends to use that knowledge to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra. The vault is an instrument. The Stone is the goal.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:16: The 500,000 gold dragons matter for what they buy: a private army, bribed magisters, time. Two Masked Lords on the Council are already compromised — Corylus Thann through scandal, Jelenn Urmbrusk through debt — and voting Manshoon's way without knowing why their interests have aligned. This leverage is his most carefully guarded intelligence. A character who brings evidence of the blackmail to Laeral Silverhand or the full Lords' Council collapses Manshoon's political position immediately, which is why the documentation lives at Kolat Towers and nowhere else.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:18: He keeps a simulacrum in Kolat Towers as his contingency against assassination, and it fights as his decoy while he escapes. His insurance against dying is the stasis clone spell, which is a separate tool. His approach reflects his temperament: precision, patience, no unnecessary violence. The Zhentarim response teams are the weakest in raw muscle of any faction. This is deliberate. Drawing too much attention is the one mistake Manshoon won't make twice.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:22: - **Manshoon** — Vaasan human wizard (clone), lawful evil. See the **Manshoon** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:23: - **Sidra Romeir** — Calishite human veteran, lawful evil. Manshoon's most trusted lieutenant, stationed at Kolat Towers. Holds the teleport rings when lieutenants leave the Towers. If captured lieutenants are killed, Sidra gives their rings to Kaevja. See the **Sidra Romeir** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:24: - **Vevette Blackwater** (DH p. 216) — Tethyrian human swashbuckler, lawful evil. A swashbuckler with fiery red hair streaked platinum that can glow in the dark on her command. Charming in public, cruel in private. Carries *Seffia Naelryke's Visiting Card*: Seffia approached Vevette to open a Cassalanter–Zhentarim communication channel, but Vevette rebuffed her, suspecting a trap. See the **Vevette Blackwater** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:25: - **Agorn Fuoco** (DH p. 195) — Turami human bard (Manshoon lieutenant), neutral evil. A bard and hollow coward who sends his thugs into danger while hanging back. Will barter his pass-amulet to Kolat Towers for his own life without hesitation. Carries a *Report to Agorn Fuoco*: intelligence confirming that Korgstrod's duergar operate out of Orb Confectioners on Rising Ride. See the **Agorn Fuoco** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:27: - **Avareen Windrivver** — Illuskan human spy (Zhentarim interrogator), lawful evil. Spy maintaining the Interrogation House alongside Zorbog. Carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers. See the **Avareen Windrivver** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:28: - **Zorbog Jyarkoth** — Turami human thug (Zhentarim), neutral evil. Thug, Avareen's partner at the Interrogation House. See the **Zorbog Jyarkoth** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:29: - **Amath Sercent** — Mulan human priest of Bane, lawful evil. Banite priestess occupying Yellowspire, allied with Manshoon. Friends with Agorn Fuoco, who visits when he wants company. See the **Amath Sercent** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:30: - **Vira Solkan** — Damaran human mage (Manshoon plant), neutral evil. See the **Vira Solkan** page in Notable Figures.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:39: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim** (Factions guide): response teams, outposts, escalation, revelation list.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:43: A ruthless Zhentarim splinter cell, wholly separate from Davil Starsong's Doom Raiders and hostile to them. The leader's name is not spoken on the street; its lean field teams move quietly through the city and take prisoners.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:68: >   - Vevette Blackwater of the Zhentarim rebuffed Seffia's approach to open a communication channel, suspecting a trap.
campaign/setting/the-grand-game/the-grand-game.md:28: - **Front Businesses:** Four factions cover their work with businesses that look ordinary. Bregan D'aerthe uses the Sea Maidens Faire, three carnival ships whose parades draw attention away from the drow's illicit activities, and the published adventure calls the Faire a front in Waterdeep. The Doom Raiders' Istrid Horn runs an illegal moneylending operation from a guarded Dock Ward warehouse, which the campaign counts among the cell's fronts. Manshoon's Zhentarim runs an importing company in the Trades Ward `[research]`, and the Cassalanters run a charitable hospital `[research]`.
campaign/setting/the-grand-game/the-grand-game.md:29: - **Informants:** The Watch has informants in every ward `[research]`, and every faction has informants in the Watch `[research]`. Jarlaxle has informants in every other faction `[research]`, and two of them are sourced. Nar'l Xibrindas, Xanathar's drow advisor, reports to him by *sending* spell, and Vessa, a doppelganger, circulates in the Cassalanters' social circle. Skeemo Weirdbottle, the Doom Raiders' Master of Magic, has been bought by House Gralhund and the Black Network operatives loyal to Manshoon, and he sends messages by paper bird both to his new friends and to his old ones.
campaign/setting/the-grand-game/the-grand-game.md:69: - **Xanathar**, **Manshoon**, **The Cassalanters** and **Jarlaxle Baenre** (Villains): the personality-phase pages for the villain factions.

campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:57: - Eye #3 began in **Renaer Neverember**'s mourning locket. Manshoon's Zhentarim seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse. Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried off the Eye with the rest of the back office without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it that night. It reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout, and Manshoon's cell never held it (**Running the Villains**; the **Xanathar Sewer Hideout** location journal, area overview). He keeps it in his stateroom, U4, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* (the **Sea Maidens Faire** location journal, area overview).
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:106: | Manshoon | Mutual wariness | Non-interference arrangement through Acts I to III, fragile, which ends when Manshoon's strike team hits the *Eyecatcher* at **Sea Maidens Faire**, whatever the party does; Jarlaxle has better intelligence and intends to keep it that way |
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:114: - Each of Jarlaxle and Manshoon considers the other the only genuine peer in the Grand Game. Manshoon has identified Fenerus Stormcastle as a Bregan D'aerthe cut-out and ordered his abduction, and Jarlaxle will act unilaterally if the abduction proceeds. That is a warning step. The arrangement ends in any case when Manshoon's strike team hits the *Eyecatcher* at **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:121: - **Hold one Eye of the Stone.** He wants it as a bargaining chip, not for personal use. Method: he already holds Eye #3 aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* (Bregan D'aerthe agents inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the night Xanathar's Guild raided the Zhentarim warehouse), and Nar'l's intelligence tells him where the other Eyes are. He keeps it as the bargaining chip and moves on the others before the other factions consolidate. Contingency: negotiating it from the PCs is also acceptable and preferable for the relationship. Connects to **Sea Maidens Faire**.

campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:184: > - **Left out:** The wiki's Zaknafein raid of 1018 DR, Artemis Entreri, Kimmuriel Oblodra, the Ghost King of 1384 DR, the 1487 DR Host Tower reconstruction, the 1366 DR Calimport base and Crenshinibon, Gromph Baenre and the "third son" gloss. Also left out: the names of Luskan's High Captains, the Lantanese wizard and the nimblewright counts, the Zardoz Betrayal Pitch, Quilm, Margo Verida, Khafeyta Murzan, Malkolm Brizzenbright and Fenerus Stormcastle beyond the Manshoon note, Zelifarn, and the Alexandrian PDF 8 Eye-holder list.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:1: # Manshoon
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:7: > - This is the villain profile for Manshoon, the clone-wizard who leads the Zhentarim splinter cell known as the Splinter from Kolat Towers in the Southern Ward. It sets out his three personality phases, his relationships, his goals and the one design note that times the simulacrum reveal.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:8: > - Use it when you run any scene where Manshoon or his operatives act or react. Phase 1 covers Acts I and II, Phase 2 covers Acts II and III, and Phase 3 begins when the party enters **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:9: > - Manshoon appears in **Finding Floon**, **Trollskull Alley**, **Fireball!**, **Gralhund Villa**, **Faction Outposts**, **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, **Kolat Towers** and **Vault of Dragons**. In the first four quests the party meets only his operatives and hears hints of a master. The party gets his name at the Interrogation House in **Faction Outposts**, which is where the **Manshoon Named** Event Outcome is marked.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:10: > - **Kolat Towers** records the result of the raid as one of three Event Outcomes (**Manshoon Destroyed**, **Manshoon Simulacrum Only** or **Manshoon Alive**), and **Vault of Dragons** reads it. The **Zhentarim Warehouse** is the location journal for the Candle Lane warehouse where a Xanathar's Guild raid killed his cell. **Kolat Towers** has no location journal yet.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:11: > - The Splinter's response teams, outposts, escalation row and revelation list live in the **Manshoon's Zhentarim** page of the Factions Guide. Who the Splinter is lives in the **Manshoon's Zhentarim** page of Organizations.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:13: **Purpose:** Distinguishes Manshoon from the Doom Raiders and lets the GM portray the campaign's most intellectually dangerous villain through three personality phases, a relationship table and three goals.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:19: > Manshoon's splinter cell and Davil Starsong's Doom Raiders are entirely separate organizations. They share a name and a history and nothing else. Never conflate them in NPC dialogue. See also **Structural Rules** and **Session Zero** in the GM Guide, and **Manshoon's Zhentarim** and **The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)** in Organizations.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:21: Manshoon is a centuries-old wizard, a clone of a clone of one of the Zhentarim's founders, who claims as every surviving clone does to be the one true Manshoon. He runs the Splinter, a cell of his own, from the warded towers of Kolat Towers in the Southern Ward. His core motive is to take the Stone of Golorr for the Weave knowledge inside it, absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra, with the vault's gold paying for the preparations.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:25: - Manshoon was born in Zhentil Keep in 1229 DR and founded the Zhentarim, also called the Black Network, in 1261 DR (Forgotten Realms Wiki, citing *The Grand History of the Realms* and *Ruins of Zhentil Keep*; the wiki does not tag their editions). The published adventure calls the original "one of the founders", and the campaign keeps that wording (**The Founding of Chains**).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:27: - In 1370 DR Fzoul Chembryl and Lord Orgauth killed him and found themselves facing a dozen or more clones. Each clone believed he was the real Manshoon and felt driven to kill the others, and the fights are the Manshoon Wars (Forgotten Realms Wiki, 3e *Forgotten Realms Campaign Setting* p. 283). The published adventure says a mishap woke every clone at once (5e *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist*).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:28: - Three clones survived the wars. One rejoined the Zhentarim and conceded leadership to Fzoul, one became the vampire Orbakh in Westgate, and one took refuge in Undermountain with Halaster (Forgotten Realms Wiki, 3e *Forgotten Realms Campaign Setting* pp. 282-283). The published adventure agrees that at least three survive and says the clone now in Waterdeep is the one that hid in Undermountain (5e *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist*).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:29: - Halaster Blackcloak captured the Undermountain clone after a brief spell duel and amputated his left arm at the elbow. Magic could not regrow it, so Manshoon crafted an artificial arm and hand for himself (5e *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist*). The severed forearm remains in Undermountain as an undead guardian (5e *Waterdeep: Dungeon of the Mad Mage*, area 1a).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:30: - The campaign makes the man in Waterdeep a clone of a clone, a body activated in Undermountain when the Manshoon before it died, as the clone network was designed to do. Halaster tolerated him for a time out of professional curiosity before taking the arm, and the clone fled upward carrying a staff of power he had smuggled in (**Kolat Towers** quest opener).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:31: - Kolat Towers is a crumbling pair of towers in the Southern Ward, built by the Kolat brothers and abandoned years ago. A force field with the properties of a wall of force still surrounds them. Manshoon rarely leaves, and he uses a teleportation circle when he must, so nobody sees him enter or leave (5e *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist*; **Kolat Towers** quest opener).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:32: - Neighbors can see the force field and the light of *continual flame* spells in the windows, and most believe the towers are abandoned or haunted by the Kolat brothers. Manshoon posts no visible guards because he wants that belief to hold (5e *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist*).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:33: - He spent about two years building the Splinter, a cell that bears his name and works independently of the Zhentarim's surviving leadership. It is hub and spoke, with Kolat Towers as the command center and the Interrogation House in the North Ward and Yellowspire in the Castle Ward as field offices linked by teleportation circles. A pass-amulet system lets him revoke anyone's access from the towers (**Kolat Towers** quest opener).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:34: - He took a large part of the local network the Doom Raiders had built, and through it learned that Lord Neverember had used the local Zhentarim's resources to embezzle the vault's gold (Alexandrian Remix, *The Villains*, p. 16).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:36: - His cell then seized Renaer Neverember's mourning locket on the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse. Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried the Eye off without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it that night. It became Eye #3, and Manshoon holds no Eye (**Running the Villains**).
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:38: - The published adventure gives him a different goal. He wants to oust Laeral Silverhand as Open Lord by bribing and blackmailing the Masked Lords, replace the City Watch with Black Network mercenaries, kill the Blackstaff, and then turn on Halaster (5e *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist*). The campaign's motive is the Weave goal under Goals, which comes from the campaign's own pages and has no published or Alexandrian source, and the blackmail of the Masked Lords stays.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:40: - He can have only one simulacrum at a time, and he uses it as a subordinate to command Zhentarim minions in the field. If it is destroyed he makes another, and the snow figure in the sarcophagus is part of the material component. He customized the spell to give the copy more hit points and less spellcasting (5e *Waterdeep: Dragon Heist*). The campaign's simulacrum rests ice-preserved in area E7 of Kolat Towers, with half his hit points and no spell slots of 6th level or higher (**Kolat Towers** quest opener).

campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:65: | The Cassalanters | Missed opportunity | Manshoon is as suspicious of their wealth and spotless name as everyone else, and an arrangement with them could have given him leverage; he chose independence over shared control `[research]` |
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:69: - Urstul Floxin ran the Dalakhar capture attempt and the **Gralhund Villa** operation. In the published adventure he takes his orders from Manshoon's simulacrum, and in the campaign he cannot name Manshoon, because he knows only a code and a chain of dead drops.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:70: - The Doom Raiders reached Waterdeep first. Manshoon's cell came later, peeled off part of their network, and now works to dismantle their people and everything they built. A party that allies with the Zhentarim through Davil works with the Doom Raiders and never with Manshoon.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:71: - The war with Xanathar is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward. Xanathar's kill order on Manshoon has failed twice, and Manshoon knows Xanathar killed his envoys and kept Eye #1.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:72: - Manshoon's parallel strike in **Sea Maidens Faire** always fires, and it sends his agents against Jarlaxle's ships in Jarlaxle's own harbor.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:80: - **Acquire the Stone of Golorr.** The Stone is a transformed aboleth encoded with ancient Weave knowledge, and Manshoon means to absorb the Weave and supplant Mystra. The vault's gold funds the process. Method: Stone recovery, then Weave absorption. He holds no Eye, because Xanathar killed the envoys who delivered Eye #1 as a gift and kept it, so he needs the Stone plus all three Eyes. Contingency: destroy the vault rather than cede it. Connects to **Gralhund Villa** and **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:82: - **Survive an Assassination.** The simulacrum is his contingency against assassination and his tactical decoy: it fights while he escapes. Defeating the Manshoon the party meets first may defeat only the simulacrum, and if it is destroyed he makes another. The stasis clone spell is a separate tool, his insurance against dying for good. Connects to the **Kolat Towers** design requirement.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:90: Zhentarim crews with flying snake tattoos work the back streets of Waterdeep. The Watch has arrested a few of them after street brawls. Their teams take prisoners, and the crews will not say who gives the orders.
campaign/setting/villains/manshoon.md:92: **Tone:** Plain and procedural for the GM tables, and guarded in the Overview, where the Zhentarim are a street presence with no face. Play Manshoon as patient and courteous, and let the clone, the Weave and the simulacrum surface only through what the party finds.

campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:16: Xanathar is a beholder and the crime lord of **Xanathar's Guild**, a paranoid intelligence that has built a criminal empire around a terror of being vulnerable and wears the trappings of a monster because the trappings are convenient. Its core motive is to prove its supremacy and remove every threat to it, which means taking the Vault of Dragons, destroying Manshoon and keeping Skullport's money flowing.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:50: | Manshoon | Rival | Active enemy; Xanathar has ordered Manshoon killed twice; both failed; this is accelerating the paranoia |
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:57: - The war with Manshoon is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward, grinding on with the kill order that has failed twice.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:62: - **Eliminate Manshoon.** The splinter cell incursion is a personal insult. Method: Guild response teams targeting Zhentarim operations. There is no contingency, and this failure is part of what drives Phase 3.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:94: - **Manshoon** and **Jarlaxle Baenre** (Villains): the rival and the unidentified player in the Relationships table
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:107: > - **Settled:** The Manshoon relationship is an active, low-level street war in the Dock Ward, and `running-the-villains.md` line 71 now says the same.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:35: That arrangement has fallen apart. Neverember is in Neverwinter, and none of the three Eyes is where he left it. Jarlaxle Baenre holds the Eye from Renaer's locket aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, where it serves as leverage and curiosity in equal measure. Manshoon's cell seized the locket the night before Ches 1, Xanathar's Guild raided the Candle Lane warehouse where it was opened, and Bregan D'aerthe agents lifted the Eye from the Guild's hideout before dawn. The Cassalanter family, working through a network of cult agents and infernal diviners, located the Eye in the crypt near Alethea Brandath's tomb and extracted it. Xanathar holds the Neverwinter Eye in Sylgar's fishbowl in the lair beneath Skullport. Zhentarim envoys stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter and carried it to Xanathar as a gift. Xanathar killed them, kept the stone, and has never learned what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:37: Manshoon holds no Eye. His envoys carried one to Xanathar and never came back. He wants the Stone itself — specifically, the compressed aboleth memory within it, which encodes structural knowledge of the Weave that Mystra's servants have never catalogued. The vault is an instrument he would use to fund his actual goal. The Stone is the goal. He needs the Stone and all three Eyes, but he never intended to open the vault the way Neverember did — only to hold the Stone long enough to extract what he needs.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:39: The Stone now sits with the party. The Watch crackdown following the Gralhund Villa incident has disrupted Manshoon's Brindul Alley operation, scattered the Zhentarim response teams, and landed Davil Starsong in Watch custody on circumstantial evidence. Xanathar's Guild is consolidating — pulling agents tighter, increasing surveillance. Jarlaxle watches the party with quiet interest and no urgency; he holds something they need, and he is in no hurry to discuss terms. The Cassalanters, undisturbed, continue their research. Below the city's peaceful surface, four factions know the Stone has surfaced. Three of them want it back.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:65: *The Fourth Faction.* The Stone does not produce a fourth vision, because Manshoon holds no Eye. If pressed — if the attuning character asks whether there are others — the Stone responds differently — an awareness rather than a vision. Something watches from a tower. It watches the Stone, not the vault. The impression ends abruptly; the Stone does not elaborate.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:83: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* The Alliance has Watch patrol schedules for every ward, available to the party with LA credentials at renown 3 or higher. Jalester's advice is careful and nonspecific: reconnaissance before entry, Watch contact windows, the legal calculus of unauthorized access to private premises. He is building an intelligence file on all four factions and makes no secret of wanting the party to share what they find from all outposts, not just whichever they target. He does not volunteer that the Lords' Alliance has independent surveillance on Yellowspire; the party can earn that information by demonstrating commitment to the Manshoon thread.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:87: *Savra Belabranta (Order of the Gauntlet — renown 1+).* The Gauntlet's intelligence on Manshoon comes from prior mission fallout — the Splinter's intermediary network, the checkpoint strike near the Trades Ward, circumstantial connections to Brindul Alley. Savra has not verified the address, but she knows the Splinter keeps a Trades Ward safe house and that prisoners taken from previous encounters were being directed somewhere. Her concern is justice: she wants the torture operation exposed, and she wants it done properly — meaning with survivors willing to testify. She does not mention that the Gauntlet has its own reasons for wanting Manshoon's legal exposure rather than his elimination.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:91: *Tashlyn Yafeera (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* Tashlyn has replaced Davil Starsong, who is in Watch custody on Gralhund-related charges. She is colder and more operational, and she establishes this in the first exchange: "Davil talked too much and trusted too easily. I won't make that mistake." She has been watching Yellowspire for two months and has schematics — structural layout, entry points, guard rotation estimates — which she will share when the party is ready to move, not before. Her immediate lead is tactical: every Manshoon agent carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers. Capturing one means one fewer entry point they can use. The subtext of everything Tashlyn says: she needs Manshoon neutralized because Manshoon neutralized is the precondition for Davil's release.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:117: **Manshoon's Zhentarim:** Parties who searched Urstul Floxin's effects at Gralhund Villa have documents pointing to Brindul Alley. Parties that did not can find the Interrogation House through North Ward canvassing — the safe house's traffic is unusual enough to notice if someone is looking (DC 14 Investigation over a day).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:127: *Cross-Faction Spillover.* Xanathar and Manshoon are actively at war; each monitors the other's losses. Hitting a Xanathar outpost raises Manshoon's alert tier by 1, and vice versa — both factions assume the other is responsible before they conclude it was the party. Cassalanter imp surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire creates a reciprocal intelligence channel; Cassalanter losses alert Jarlaxle that something is moving against infernal-adjacent operations in the city. See **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for full inter-faction dynamics.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:165: *Approach.* Grinda operates openly as a black-market armorer. Anyone asking about Xanathar weapons, Guild suppliers, or black-market arms in the Dock Ward gets pointed to Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma in Dock Ward taverns or street contacts; no faction membership required). Three additional paths: the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Terasse Estate names her; a captured Xanathar response team member's *Directive from Nar'l* names her as a supplier; Agorn Fuoco's *Report to Agorn Fuoco* (from any Zhentarim response team) confirms her location. Parties can approach as customers, investigators, or raiders. Grinda does not fight for the Guild — she fights for her business. She will negotiate if the party offers more immediate value than Xanathar does, and she will report the encounter afterward.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:191: ### Scene 5: Manshoon's Zhentarim Outposts
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:193: **Purpose:** Two outposts that define Manshoon's character through contrast. The Interrogation House is ugly and deliberate — a place where precision curdles into cruelty. Yellowspire is quieter and stranger — a Banite tower hiding in plain sight in the Castle Ward. Together they deliver the campaign's first named reference to Manshoon and the only bypass route to Kolat Towers that avoids the force field.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:195: **Tone:** Espionage thriller. Manshoon's operations are surgical. The Interrogation House is the exception — deliberately ugly, reminding the party that his patience has limits. Yellowspire is quieter, stranger, a Banite tower in plain sight.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:201: *North Ward, Brindul Alley.* — **Street-level entry point for the Manshoon chain** (for parties with **Gralhund Villa** documents).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:203: *Approach.* Four paths to the Interrogation House: the *Directive to Urstul Floxin* from **Gralhund Villa** names Avareen Windrivver and Brindul Alley explicitly; Yellowspire's crumpled note from Avareen (for parties who hit Yellowspire first); questioning a captured Zhentarim response team member (prisoners taken by Zhent teams are brought here — they'll say so); or North Ward canvassing for parties lacking **Gralhund Villa** documents (DC 14 Investigation over a day; no faction membership required). The house appears unremarkable from the street. Use house L1 layout (DH p. 62).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:207: *The Directive to Zorbog.* A flying snake arrives during or shortly after the party enters, bearing a sealed letter addressed to Zorbog. The letter is a *Directive to Zorbog* signed **Manshoon** — the first document in the campaign bearing his name as a signature. It orders the kidnap of Fenerus Stormcastle of Quill Alley, specifying delivery to the Interrogation House within four days. The party now knows: there is a named Manshoon. He is the Splinter's leader. He is targeting a specific civilian. And his directive's phrasing suggests awareness of the party's recent activities.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:211: *Intel Yield.* Manshoon's name (first use in the campaign). The pass-amulet from Avareen (route to Kolat Towers). Ott Steeltoes as a potential **Xanathar's Lair** contact. The *Directive to Zorbog* naming Fenerus Stormcastle — a cross-faction thread pointing toward Bregan D'aerthe (Fenerus is a BD lamplighter; Manshoon has identified him as a soft target).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:213: **Escalation: Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3).** Avareen's flying snakes carry the alarm faster than the party can move; if even one escapes, Manshoon knows within hours. Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 1d4 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves directly to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:227: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Primary objective: locate and document the permanent teleportation circle to Kolat Towers. The circle is active. Stepping through it immediately means arriving at Kolat Towers unannounced and unprepared — a one-way trip that bypasses the force field, which is Yellowspire's entire value to Manshoon. Secondary: a crumpled note from Avareen, cross-referencing the Interrogation House. Tertiary: Agorn's pass-amulet to Kolat Towers, which he surrenders without hesitation in exchange for his life.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:231: *Step 5 — Getaway.* Amath fights with conviction; acolytes are less committed. Agorn flees. **Escalation: Manshoon moves to Suspicious (Tier 2) if the Interrogation House has not been hit; moves to Lockdown (Tier 4) if it has.** Agorn carries word to surviving Zhentarim contacts if he escapes. Response teams redeploy within 1d4 days.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:275: *Step 1 — Gather Information.* Five paths to the Converted Windmill: Seffia Naelryke's visiting card found on Vevette Blackwater from a Zhentarim response team (Seffia approached Vevette to open a communication channel; Vevette rebuffed her, suspecting a trap, but kept the card); Arn's Book of Worship at the Asmodean Shrine; the *Injury Reports* logbook at Terasse Estate ("the windmill on Coachlamp Lane"); a Cassalanter Villa report if **Cassalanter Villa** is completed first; or the infernal presence detected by Divine Sense within 60 feet of the building (DC 12 Wisdom for any character with Divine Sense; no faction required). Parties without Divine Sense can reach the windmill through Coachlamp Lane neighborhood inquiry — neighbors report strange smells, unusual visitors after midnight, a couple who never quite fit in (DC 13 Charisma; no faction required).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:303: *Approach.* Three paths to Fenerus: the Seven Masks Theater intelligence archive (a letter from "K" to "J" referencing Fenerus as a BD field contact and his address at Quill Alley); the *Directive to Zorbog* from the Interrogation House (Manshoon is targeting Fenerus — his address is in the directive); or a captured BD response team member's *Sealed Letter from Krebbyg*, naming Fenerus as a message recipient.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:309: If the party did not warn him and arrives before the kidnap team: Zorbog's team may be in the alley or entering the building. A three-way encounter develops — party, Zhentarim kidnappers, and Fenerus in the middle.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:351: *Response Team Compositions.* See the relevant Factions guide pages for full compositions and carried documents. Every team leader carries faction-specific documents that function as intelligence opportunities: Zhentarim teams carry directives naming Brindul Alley or Kolat Towers; Xanathar teams carry operational correspondence from Nar'l or Ahmaergo; BD teams carry playbills or coded messages in Drow; Cassalanter teams carry instructions from Lord Cassalanter. The party should treat every response team encounter as a potential document recovery, not just a combat encounter.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:353: *Cross-Faction Response Team Encounters.* Xanathar and Manshoon teams may encounter each other during this quest — both factions are moving through the same wards. Witnessing these confrontations without engaging either side generates intelligence about both factions simultaneously. BD teams prefer misdirection over lethal force; they are more likely to shadow the party than confront them directly. Cassalanter teams vary by the party's relationship with the family.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:371: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance).* Wants all outpost intelligence shared with the Watch, framed as actionable intelligence about criminal operations rather than unauthorized entry. Offers legal cover for all this quest's operations retroactively in exchange for Manshoon intelligence that will hold up in a Magisters' proceeding. He is building a legal case, not an arrest warrant; the distinction matters to him.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:377: *Vajra Safahr (Force Grey).* Asks about arcane intelligence: teleportation circles, magical defenses, ward schematics. If the Yellowspire teleportation circle was documented, she immediately requests the sigil sequence. Vajra can verify the circle's destination (Kolat Towers) through divination and will confirm it within a day of receiving the sequence. She shares that the Blackstaff has been tracking Manshoon's arcane signature and can use the sigil sequence to triangulate his operational base. She does not share what she intends to do with this information.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:379: *Tashlyn Yafeera (Doom Raiders).* Asks about Manshoon's operational capacity. If pass-amulets were captured, she wants to examine them — specifically to determine whether they contain identification components that could be used to track the holder's movements. If the Interrogation House was hit, she wants to know whether Davil's name appears in any recovered documents. Her concern is documented and unstated in equal measure: she is building a legal defense for Davil and needs evidence that Doom Raiders involvement in the Gralhund operation was circumstantial rather than directed.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:393: *Completing a chain:* Hit at least two outposts of the same faction and recover at least one piece of actionable lair intelligence. For Xanathar (three outposts), two of three is sufficient. For all other factions (two outposts), both must be cleared. A party that hits one Xanathar outpost and one Zhentarim outpost has started two chains but completed neither.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:413: 5. **Player faction hooks gap-filled.** Enclave: infernal resonance confirmed as infernal but source unknown (→ Cassalanters). Gauntlet: Splinter intelligence trail (→ Manshoon). Force Grey: BD operational infrastructure (→ theater and message architecture).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:417: 7. **Manshoon reveal sequence.** Name only at Interrogation House (*Directive to Zorbog*). Identity at **Kolat Towers**. Motivation at **Vault of Dragons**.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:469: ***Cross-Faction Spillover and the Xanathar–Manshoon War.*** The spillover rules — hitting Xanathar raises Manshoon's tier and vice versa — have a specific purpose beyond realism. They ensure that a party pursuing only one faction's chain still generates meaningful pressure across multiple fronts.
