A party that focuses entirely on Xanathar outposts will encounter Manshoon response teams without having engaged Manshoon directly, surfacing the inter-faction war as lived experience rather than exposition.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:477: ## The Manshoon Reveal Sequence
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:479: ***Name, Identity, Motivation.*** Manshoon's revelation has been staged across three quests: name at the Interrogation House, identity at **Kolat Towers**, motivation at **Vault of Dragons**. The decision to surface his name on a document that arrives live during the party's operation — rather than in a cutscene or NPC speech — is deliberate. The *Directive to Zorbog* bearing his signature is the party's first tangible evidence that the Splinter has a named leader. It lands differently there, in a house they just broke into, while a prisoner they may or may not rescue is strapped to a chair nearby, than it would in a faction briefing. The emotional environment matters.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:495: **Harpers.** This quest's Harper hook is an ongoing operative benefit rather than a single scene beat. Once the party reaches Renown 30+, Mirt can direct embedded Harper informants to provide advance intelligence against Xanathar and Manshoon outposts — shift rotations, guard counts, unusual personnel — delivered before each operation rather than discovered during it. A separate informant inside the Sea Maidens Faire reports that Jarlaxle has been asking about the party by description; Mirt passes this to Harper PCs as a quiet heads-up in the days before **Sea Maidens Faire** begins. These benefits are not surfaced in Scene 2's consultation; they activate through maintained renown and are delivered as one informant report at the start of each outpost operation the party undertakes in Scenes 4–7, once a Harper PC has Renown 30+. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Harpers** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:9: Three months before the Trollskull fireball, Manshoon dispatched two Zhentarim lieutenants to Skullport carrying a gift: Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr, stolen from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. The Eye was offered as a peace overture — proof that Manshoon's splinter faction could be a valuable partner in the Grand Game. Xanathar received the envoys in its throne room, listened to their proposal, and ate them. It kept the Eye and has never learned what it is. The Eye passed to Ott Steeltoes, who placed it in Sylgar's fishbowl because it had a pretty color and Xanathar liked Sylgar's bowl to look interesting.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:13: Nihiloor has used this paranoia as cover. The illithid's three unauthorized projects — running in parallel for years — are now approaching critical phases. The Infiltration: intellect devourers inserted into four positions in Waterdeep's Watch and civic administration, with two additional placements pending. The Experiments: twelve captives from the gladiatorial arena modified into psionic relay organisms, intended to form the backbone of a city-wide intelligence network that would answer only to Nihiloor. The Puppet: four months ago, Nihiloor successfully applied Dominate Monster to Xanathar through a sustained psionic relay device in X26. The Xanathar who has been running the Guild since then — the Xanathar who killed Manshoon's envoys, who sent response teams against the party's allies in **Faction Outposts**, who holds the Eye in a fishbowl because it trusts Ott's aesthetic judgment — has been Nihiloor's instrument. The beholder does not know this. The domination does not feel like domination; it feels like its own decisions.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:33: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* Watch patrol schedules for the sewer approaches to Skullport: three viable windows per day in the Dock Ward drainage network. If Manshoon outposts were also hit in **Faction Outposts** (cross-faction spillover raising the Watch's general alert posture), the gaps narrow. Jalester can identify a 90-minute window on the third watch that still exists even at elevated patrol density. At renown 10+, Jalester provides a letter of introduction to the Dock Ward senior Watch officer, who will formally log any disturbance report the party files — giving their sewer approach a legal cover story and keeping Watch patrols clear of the guildsign entrance for two hours.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:147: **X7 — Cell Block.** Eight cells cut into rock, three occupied. Samara (gladiatorial combatant, six weeks in captivity) knows: Xanathar holds "a magic stone and a marble with a colored center"; the marble is in "the fish tank in the big room with the purple carpet"; and Manshoon's organization is based in a tower in the North Ward (overheard from another prisoner since sent to the arena). The tower information is one of three clue paths to **Kolat Towers**. Two Guild Soldiers watch the cell gate; a third is asleep in a chair. Cell locks are DC 13 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools). If all three prisoners are freed, the two Guild members in custody offer to start a fight at X2 — buying the party 5 minutes before being subdued.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:167: **X33 — Crypt of Xanathar's Past.** Through the secret door from X6 (DC 17 Perception), a narrow passage leads to a chamber containing the preserved remains and personal effects of every previous beholder who held the title "Xanathar" — predecessors stretching back centuries. Nar'l Xibrindas is the only current Guild member who knows this room exists. The room contains: evidence that "Xanathar" is a title claimed through murder, not inherited; the original Guild charter signed by the first holder; and locked coffer containing the personal journals of the last three Xanathars, including early dealings with Manshoon's predecessors. The revelation that Xanathar is a title implies the current one could be replaced, and that someone may already be planning to do so.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:175: - **X23 — Competitive Intelligence Files.** Nihiloor's research on all four villain factions, assembled as preparation for an eventual play for true control. The Manshoon section is the most detailed: intercepted Zhentarim communications, field reports on Kolat Towers' external security, a partial lieutenant roster, and a ground-level map of Kolat Towers. This is one of three clue paths to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:286: **Manshoon's Zhentarim Splinter.** Monitoring via agents watching the lair's entrances. If Xanathar is weakened or eliminated, Manshoon moves within days to absorb abandoned Guild territory. His alert tier increases if the party was observed.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:344: - **Kolat Towers** — Samara's testimony (X7), Nihiloor's Manshoon intelligence (X23), and a third clue seeded in **Cassalanter Villa** form the Three Clue Rule paths to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:410: The Manshoon intelligence in X23 is a designed cross-quest clue. Three independent clue paths lead to **Kolat Towers**: Samara's testimony in X7 (a tower in the North Ward), Nihiloor's intelligence files in X23 (partial Kolat Towers layout and lieutenant roster), and a third path seeded in **Cassalanter Villa**. The Three Clue Rule is satisfied across quests, not within a single location. This is the correct application of the rule in a campaign where quests run in variable order.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:45: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* The Cassalanters hold banking leverage over half the guilds in Waterdeep. Tashlyn or Davil can provide intelligence on the household's operations: servant rotation schedules, delivery windows, and which guild contractors have regular access to the villa grounds. The Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild has records of the villa's basement construction — partial blueprints available through a Doom Raider contact. These blueprints show the wine cellar (C28), the false vault (C27), and the original underground construction that predates the villa. A hatch between C22 and C23 appears on the old plans — it was not originally concealed. The blueprints do not show C29 (later construction) or the temple interior. At renown 10+, Yagra Stonefist drives a Black Network carriage and serves as lookout outside the villa during the operation — maintaining contact through a code system (a kerchief in a window) and ready to run an immediate extraction through the Sea Ward's back streets.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:197: **C6 — Victoro's Office.** Second floor, locked (DC 17 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools) or Victoro's key). A helmed horror stands in the corner — motionless until a creature other than Victoro or Ammalia touches the desk, at which point it attacks without warning. The desk contains: thirteen loan contracts (Cassalanter banking leverage over Waterdeep's guilds), a hidden compartment (DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation)) holding a gold Asmodeus holy symbol worth 250 GP, a report from Seffia Naelryke on outpost operations (cross-reference to **Faction Outposts** Converted Windmill), and the Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game. The Report on the Grand Game is the Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers** — it references Manshoon's operations, identifies Kolat Towers by name and location, describes Zhentarim splinter movements, and notes that "the wizard who calls himself Manshoon guards his fortress with a force field that admits only those he has keyed."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:357: **Manshoon's Zhentarim.** Monitors through double agents. If the Cassalanters are weakened, Manshoon considers one fewer rival and may accelerate his own timeline.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:499: The Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is placed in both C6 (Victoro's desk) and C22 (Ammalia's study). This dual placement ensures that a party reaching either room — not necessarily both — discovers the path to Kolat Towers. The report references Manshoon by operational name, identifies the Towers' location, describes Zhentarim splinter activity, and notes the force-field entry restriction. It is the third of three independent paths to **Kolat Towers**, alongside Samara's testimony from **Xanathar's Lair** X7 and Nihiloor's intelligence files from **Xanathar's Lair** X23.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:160: **J30 (Zardoz Zord's Cabin).** Jarlaxle's private quarters aboard the Eyecatcher. 1 nimblewright valet. Contains: the Nimblewright Sales Ledger (complete list of nine buyers — cross-reference **Fireball!** Scene 4 for the partial ledger the party may already hold); the Report on the Cultists of Asmodeus (identifies the Asmodean Shrine on Aveen Street and the ancient worship site below the Sea Ward — Three Clue Rule path to **Cassalanter Villa**, if not yet completed); Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (references all four factions' positions including Manshoon's Kolat Towers — Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**). The trapdoor to the Scarlet Marpenoth is beneath a rug in the aft corner — DC 16 Perception to locate, DC 18 Thieves' Tools to unlock or Jarlaxle's key.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:198: **Purpose.** What goes wrong, what goes sideways, and how the party gets out — managed alongside the Manshoon parallel strike and the Jarlaxle confrontation.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:202: *The Manshoon Parallel Strike.* Manshoon knows Eye #3 is aboard the Scarlet Marpenoth. His intelligence sources — scrying via simulacrum, double agents in multiple factions, Zhentarim informants in the Dock Ward — have confirmed the target. He dispatches a strike team regardless of what path the party has chosen.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:206: **Trigger Conditions.** The Manshoon strike always fires. On the heist path, the strike begins 30 minutes after the party boards the Eyecatcher. On the alliance or patron path, the strike hits while the party is aboard negotiating or receiving the Eye — Jarlaxle's crew repels the Zhentarim, but the attack gives Jarlaxle a reason to ask the party for payback: "They came to my ship. In my harbor. I want to know where they came from, and I want it to stop." This becomes a direct hook into **Kolat Towers** — Jarlaxle sends the party after Manshoon at Kolat Towers, providing whatever BD intelligence the network has on the location. The Zhent attack transforms the alliance and patron paths from clean resolutions into obligations with teeth.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:210: | PC State | Zhentarim Action | Result |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:212: | PCs undetected, moving through Eyecatcher | Zhentarim board port side, trigger alarm on J1 deck watch | Alarm draws crew AWAY from PCs toward the Zhentarim; pressure relief for the party but chaos increases |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:213: | PCs detected, improvising | Zhentarim arrive into existing chaos | Three-way: PCs vs. BD crew vs. Zhentarim. BD crew splits response. Party can exploit the confusion |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:214: | PCs in Scarlet Marpenoth | Zhentarim fight through Eyecatcher, reach J30, discover trapdoor | Race to U4 — Zhentarim are 5–10 minutes behind the party in the submarine |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:215: | PCs have Eye, escaping | Zhentarim encounter PCs during extraction | Zhentarim attempt to take the Eye from the party; BD crew may treat both groups as hostile or focus on the more visible threat |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:216: | PCs encounter Zhentarim directly | Combat or negotiation | Zhentarim will trade information (Kolat Towers intelligence, Manshoon's operations) for the Eye. They will not accept anything less. If defeated, their pass-amulets to Kolat Towers are recoverable — Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers** |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:217: | PCs allied or patron path (not heisting) | Zhentarim board and attack BD crew | Jarlaxle's crew fights back; party can assist or stand clear. Jarlaxle uses the attack as leverage: "Now you owe me — find where they came from." Pass-amulets recovered from defeated Zhentarim + Jarlaxle's BD intelligence on Manshoon = strongest **Kolat Towers** entry path available |
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:219: *Facing Jarlaxle.* If Jarlaxle confronts the party — because the alarm reached him, because the Zhentarim strike drew him out, or because he was in J30 or J32 when the party arrived:
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:239: **Tone.** The complications should feel like a heist going sideways — not catastrophic, but requiring rapid adaptation. Jarlaxle's confrontation should feel like a chess match, not a battle. The Manshoon strike adds genuine danger — the Zhentarim are not charming; they are violent, and their presence transforms the caper into something more dangerous. The contrast between Jarlaxle's wit and the Zhentarim's brutality is the scene's defining dynamic.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:278: **Manshoon's Zhentarim.** If the Zhentarim strike team was defeated, Manshoon knows — the pass-amulets went dark. He adjusts **Kolat Towers** preparations. If the strike team caused chaos but failed to get the Eye, Manshoon redirects to Kolat Towers defense. Manshoon's cell holds no Eye unless the party's failure puts one there: if the strike team recovered the Eye, **Kolat Towers** becomes a recovery operation as well as an assault.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:293: - *Tashlyn or Davil (Doom Raiders):* "What happened to the Zhentarim team?" Names and faces from the strike team help them map Manshoon's remaining force structure. The pass-amulets to Kolat Towers are of particular interest: "Those get you inside his base. That is extremely valuable intelligence."
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:302: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone with 1–3 Eyes. The remaining lair heists are available in any order. The Report on Cultists of Asmodeus (J30) points toward **Cassalanter Villa** if not yet completed. The Letter from Nar'l (U3) confirms the BD/Xanathar connection, providing context for **Xanathar's Lair**. Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (if recovered from Scene 5) both point toward **Kolat Towers**. If this was Eye #3 — the Full Awakening — **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:304: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. The caper's resolution carries a different emotional register than **Xanathar's Lair**'s revelatory awakening or **Cassalanter Villa**'s moral reckoning. This quest's aftermath is about relationships — with Jarlaxle (enemy, rival, or ally?), with Zelifarn (the crystal ball decision), with Manshoon's Zhentarim (confirmed threat, now owed payback). The party has engaged with the most charming villain in the Grand Game. What that engagement produced depends entirely on how they played it.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:342: - **Kolat Towers** — Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (Scene 5) both provide independent Three Clue Rule paths to **Kolat Towers**. Combined with Jarlaxle's BD intelligence on Manshoon (alliance/patron path), this is the strongest entry path to **Kolat Towers** in the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:360: This quest is the only lair heist that offers three fundamentally different approaches to obtaining the Eye. This is because Jarlaxle is the only villain who would willingly trade it for something he values more. Xanathar does not negotiate — it eats envoys. The Cassalanters are too desperate — the children's deadline consumes their attention. Manshoon holds no Eye. Jarlaxle is the one Grand Game villain who treats every interaction as a transaction, and the Eye is currency he is willing to spend if the price is right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:384: ## Manshoon Parallel Strike as Rival Actor
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:386: Every lair heist needs a parallel heist actor: **Xanathar's Lair** has Jarlaxle's simultaneous heist, **Cassalanter Villa** has Esvele Rosznar, this quest has Manshoon's Zhentarim strike team. The parallel actor creates three-way pressure, provides a pressure-relief valve, and connects the current quest to future quests.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:388: Manshoon is the correct choice for this quest because: he has the intelligence resources (scrying, double agents) to know the Eye's location; his Zhentarim are the most violent faction, creating genuine danger contrast against Jarlaxle's charm; the strike team's pass-amulets to Kolat Towers provide a Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**; and on the alliance or patron path, the Zhent attack gives Jarlaxle a personal reason to send the party after Manshoon — transforming a clean diplomatic resolution into one that carries a new obligation and provides the strongest possible **Kolat Towers** entry.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:390: The strike always fires regardless of which resolution path the party chose. Manshoon's intelligence is independent of the party's relationship with Jarlaxle. This is a deliberate departure from **Cassalanter Villa**'s Esvele matrix, where the parallel actor could be avoided entirely — Manshoon is more aggressive and more consequential.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:426: **To Kolat Towers.** Two independent paths from this quest: Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) references Manshoon's operations and Kolat Towers; and the Zhentarim pass-amulets recovered from the Manshoon strike team (Scene 5) provide physical access to Kolat Towers. These join the existing paths from **Xanathar's Lair** (Samara's testimony from X7, Nihiloor's files from X23) and **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game from C6 or C22). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied to **Kolat Towers** through any combination of prior quests.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:13: The Manshoon who now occupies Kolat Towers is a clone of a clone — a body activated in Undermountain when the Manshoon before it died, as the clone network was designed to do. Halaster Blackcloak, who tolerated the clone for a time out of professional curiosity, eventually severed Manshoon's left arm at the elbow with a blade that prevented magical regrowth. The clone fled Undermountain carrying the staff of power he had smuggled in and arrived in Waterdeep to find the Kolat Towers abandoned, the force field still running on its original enchantment, and the amulet manufacture instructions in Duhlark's study. Manshoon needed a base. The towers were available.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:15: He spent two years building the Splinter cell — the faction that bears his name but operates independently of the Zhentarim's surviving leadership. The structure is hub-and-spoke: Kolat Towers as the command center, with the Interrogation House in the North Ward and Yellowspire in the Castle Ward serving as field offices connected by teleportation circles. A pass-amulet system replaced the need for a trusted courier network; amulets could be revoked remotely, confiscating access without confrontation. The towers' extradimensional sanctum — a space behind the towers that is not physically behind the towers, accessible only via a teleportation ring keyed to a specific circle in K22 — houses Manshoon's closest lieutenants and his most sensitive operations.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:17: The simulacrum in E7 is Manshoon's contingency against assassination. It was created seven years ago and has been ice-preserved since — a duplicate with half his hit points, none of his 6th-level or higher spell slots, and a different quality to its decision-making that those who know Manshoon well can identify after the fact. The simulacrum has never been activated outside of tests. Manshoon has never needed it. He is considering, for the first time in years, whether he might.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:19: The blackmail operation is what funds the Splinter's political reach. Corylus Thann, a Masked Lord, was photographed in a situation that would end his career; he supplies Guild intelligence in exchange for the ledger's continued privacy. Jelenn Urmbrusk accumulated significant debt to a Splinter front organization; her votes in the Council of Guildmasters follow Manshoon's preferences without her ever being asked directly. The ledger documenting these arrangements, and three others, lives in an arcane-locked drawer in Manshoon's study in E13. The identity list in the same drawer names Mirt, Thardouk Starbuckler, and Dorgar Adarbrent as Masked Lords — intelligence Manshoon has been accumulating for reasons that are not yet clear.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:25: **Purpose.** Convert prior-arc intelligence — the name "Manshoon," the Kolat Towers location, the pass-amulet system, the Splinter network — into an operational picture of a fortified position. Unlike **Xanathar's Lair** (a dungeon whose defenses the party can infiltrate around) or **Cassalanter Villa** (a villa with social cover) or **Sea Maidens Faire** (a carnival that looks like something other than a military installation), this briefing reveals a target that is overtly what it is: a wizard's fortress behind an impenetrable force field. The party cannot sneak up on Kolat Towers. They can only prepare better or worse for the assault.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:33: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* Harper agents have been watching the Kolat Towers block since **Faction Outposts**, tracking lieutenant movement. Mirt provides: exterior description and confirmed ward location (Southern Ward, not the North Ward — the misdirection in Samara's **Xanathar's Lair** testimony was fragmentary field intelligence); pass-amulet delivery patterns (Manshoon's couriers approach from two directions, suggesting two off-site entry points); the observation that the force field extends twelve feet above the roof and six feet below street level, making aerial approaches impossible and undermining impossible without specialized tools; and a warning that Watch patrol coverage in the surrounding streets has been quietly reduced over the past eighteen months — someone with civic influence arranged it. At renown 10+, Mirt activates a 48-hour Harper surveillance rotation: a field agent (spy stat block) delivers a written report covering patrol patterns by time of day, delivery schedules, and one verified force-field gap cycle including the gap's precise location, duration, and rotation frequency.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:45: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle's interest in Kolat Towers is personal. If **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Manshoon parallel strike fired — and it always fires — Manshoon sent agents to attack Jarlaxle's ships in his own harbor. The experience left Jarlaxle more annoyed than alarmed, which for him is the more dangerous emotional state. "They came to my ship. In my harbor. I want to know where they came from, and I want it to stop." BD intelligence on Kolat Towers is ground-floor-only — the Bregan D'aerthe never penetrated past the second floor of the towers and have no information on the sanctum. What Jarlaxle provides: partial interior layout (K1 through K9), confirmation of the pass-amulet system by name and mechanism, confirmation that Sidra Romeir commands internal security, and the observation that Manshoon's agents carry amulets minted with a specific design that distinguishes them from amulets issued to the towers' service staff. If the party recovered pass-amulets from the defeated Zhentarim strike team during **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Scene 5, Jarlaxle confirms they work — those amulets bypass the force field and unlock the front door. At renown 10+, Jarlaxle provides one additional piece: "The wizard in those towers lost an arm in Undermountain. Whatever he is — and I have suspicions — he came up from below." The Halaster connection is stated without elaboration. It is the first hint that Manshoon's identity is stranger than a Zhentarim faction leader.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:57: *Identifying the Score.* Kolat Towers holds no Eye. The Stone of Golorr offers no impression pointing to this location. The party's motivation is strategic: neutralize Manshoon before he ambushes them at the Vault, recover the blackmail ledger, seize intelligence that makes **Vault of Dragons** viable. This is the only lair heist where the party might legitimately ask "why are we doing this?" The answer comes from three convergent directions. First, every faction that provides intelligence about Manshoon eventually ends the same way — "and he will not stop." The ledger, the Vault approach intelligence, the political reach — leaving Manshoon operational means arriving at **Vault of Dragons** against an adversary who has had weeks to prepare. Second, Lady Gondafrey. Savra's briefing makes her existence and her location known; the party knows there is a prisoner in K13 who has been there for 113 years. Third, the intelligence haul itself has specific quest consequences that every contact will name if pressed. "The Blackstaff wants that spellbook. The Open Lord wants that ledger. Whatever Manshoon knows about the Vault, you want to know it first." The party should arrive at Scene 4 having decided what they are there to get.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:62: - Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (**Cassalanter Villa**): references Manshoon and identifies the towers' location in the Southern Ward, correcting Samara's misdirection.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:63: - Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (**Sea Maidens Faire**, J30): references Manshoon's Splinter operations.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:64: - Zhentarim pass-amulets (**Sea Maidens Faire**, Scene 5): recovered from the defeated strike team — physical entry tokens.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:67: This quest is the convergence point for every faction's Manshoon intelligence. A party that spread across multiple quests arrives here having assembled the picture from multiple angles. A party that skipped most of those quests arrives having assembled less but still enough — Samara's testimony alone names the target.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:73: **Captured Pass-Amulets (via Avareen Windrivver, Agorn Fuoco, or the **Sea Maidens Faire** Zhentarim strike team).** Amulets recovered from any of these sources allow the party to walk through the force field to K1 (the ground-floor entrance) without triggering it. The amulets also prevent the force field's passive alarm from registering unauthorized entry. Advantage: social window — Sidra will not attack immediately on sight at Unaware or Suspicious tier, creating 1–3 minutes of ambiguous contact before the operation's nature becomes clear. Disadvantage: the approach is on the street, visible from the towers' windows. At Alert tier, captured amulets require a DC 14 Intelligence (Arcana) check to recalibrate before use — a failure produces an audible rejection tone that the garrison hears. At Lockdown tier, captured amulets are invalid; only freshly issued amulets function.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:79: **Tone.** Compressed and decisive. The raid genre imposes its own time pressure before the operation even begins. Every hour the party spends observing the towers is an hour Manshoon has to prepare. The Founders' Day clock, the Vault approach intelligence sitting in E9 and E13, and Manshoon's active **Vault of Dragons** preparations all push against extended casing. The party should feel that the optimal casing window is shorter than they would like, and that waiting longer costs something.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:91: Lady Gondafrey is a secondary objective, not a recruited ally — the party will not know her current alignment state before entering K13. Savra's briefing establishes her existence and the moral case for her extraction. What she provides if freed depends on her alignment: a lawful-good Gondafrey is the most valuable ally in the towers, with 113 years of architectural knowledge and a deeply personal motivation to see Manshoon's operation destroyed; a chaotic-evil Gondafrey is an active threat. The DC 14 Wisdom (Insight) check that detects her current state is available only after K13 is reached. The party should decide, before entering the towers, whether extracting Lady Gondafrey is a primary objective, a secondary objective, or a risk they are not willing to take.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:99: | Tier | Force Field | Garrison State | Manshoon & Simulacrum | Timer |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:101: | Unaware | Standard; gaps on normal rotation | Standard roster; Sidra assumes visitors are guests until credentials fail | Manshoon studying in E13; simulacrum dormant in E7 | 30 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:102: | Suspicious | Gaps shifted 15 min from diagram; amulets work | +2 thugs at K2; Sidra checks credentials before allowing passage | Manshoon studying in E13; simulacrum dormant in E7 | 25 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:103: | Alert | Gaps shifted 30 min; amulets require DC 14 Arcana to recalibrate | Posts doubled; K22 adds 2 thugs to standard spectator/snake garrison; Manafret poisons all kitchen food | Manshoon in E12, staff in hand; simulacrum activated in E1 as decoy | 20 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:104: | Lockdown | Gaps sealed; only fresh amulets work; Yellowspire circle still functions | Full garrison at battle stations; Sidra does not ask questions | Manshoon in E12, defensive spells prepared; simulacrum activated in E1, briefed on party capabilities via Skeemo's intelligence if he survived **Faction Outposts** | 15 min |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:116: *Running This Scene.* The critical path runs K1/K22 (entry) → K2/K3 (Sidra, Manafret, teleporter ring) → K22 (circle to sanctum) → E1/E2/E3 (sanctum foyer) → deeper sanctum → E12 or E13 (Manshoon). Everything else is optional. A party that clears every room fights a garrison that gets larger with every minute they spend. A party that moves with purpose reaches Manshoon before the timer reaches its hardest entries on the schedule. The GM communicates timer progression through environmental cues: flying snake couriers crossing between towers, shouted orders echoing from below, boots on stairs that were silent a minute ago.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:118: Jarlaxle's position and the simulacrum's state are predecided by escalation tier. At Unaware and Suspicious, Manshoon is studying in E13 and the simulacrum is dormant in E7. At Alert and Lockdown, Manshoon is in E12 with the staff of power in hand and the simulacrum is activated and standing in E1. These positions do not change during Scene 4 unless the party's actions force Manshoon to move — and Manshoon moves toward a confrontation, not away from one.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:122: **K1 — Ground-Floor Entrance.** The front door. Only relevant for pass-amulet entry. A Zhentarim thug behind a viewing port opens it if the amulet check passes, admits the visitors to the narrow entry hall, and moves to alert Sidra in K2. At Unaware tier, this happens without alarm. At Suspicious and Alert, the thug calls for Sidra before the door is fully open.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:132: **K13 — Holding Cell.** Lady Gondafrey. She has been in this room for 113 years. The alignment shift mechanic: her alignment resets at dawn each day, shifting on a d10 — but per standing campaign design rules, the GM does not roll. Her alignment for the operation is determined by when the party arrives relative to dawn: if the party enters the towers before dawn, she is at whatever state she was at last night's reset, which is chaotic-evil (Manshoon uses the alignment oscillation as a psychological tool — she is sometimes herself and sometimes not, and she cannot be certain which she is). If the party enters after dawn and within 4 hours of it, she is lawful-good. If the party enters more than 4 hours after dawn, she is drifting — treat as true neutral, cooperative but not fully capable of tactical coordination. The party's entry timing therefore determines which version of Lady Gondafrey they find. If she is lawful-good: she provides a complete verbal floor plan of both towers and the sanctum (she memorized the layout over decades), identifies the anchor in K18 by name ("the rune room — destroy the stone in the wall, the field falls"), and offers to fight alongside the party with whatever she can find. She is a skilled combatant at CR 5 equivalent (veteran stat block, with innate arcane disruption abilities from her Order training). If she is chaotic-evil: she attacks the party on sight.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:134: **K15 — Summoning Chamber.** 3 apprentice wizard minions (22 HP each) in active ritual, binding a barlgura in the summoning circle. The apprentices are the minimum complement required to maintain the binding — disrupting any one of them (dropping them below half HP or breaking their concentration) releases the demon. Released, the barlgura attacks the nearest creature, friend or enemy, for 3 rounds, then rampages through the towers following noise. The trade-off: a released barlgura draws 2–4 Zhentarim defenders from other positions to contain it, creating a tactical window for the party — but it also triggers the reinforcement timer if not already running, and poses genuine injury risk. The barlgura ignores pass-amulets.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:136: **K17 — Flesh Golem Access Corridor.** A pressure plate 10 feet before the K18 door releases a cloud of contact poison gas (DC 13 Constitution saving throw, 14 (4d6) poison damage on a failed save; the poisoned condition lasts 1 minute on failure). DC 14 Perception to notice the plate before stepping on it; DC 13 Thieves' Tools to disable it. The flesh golem in the corridor guards the approach to K18 and does not have pass-amulet discrimination — it attacks anything moving toward K18 that does not carry a specific command stone (in Manshoon's possession).
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:138: **K18 — Arcane Rune (Force Field Anchor).** A stone rune set into the east wall of a bare room — the physical anchor for the force field. The rune radiates strong abjuration. The moment it takes any damage (AC 10, 22 HP), a red slaad teleports into the room. The slaad is Manshoon's failsafe — it attacks the party until destroyed or dismissed. The slaad does not discriminate; it will attack Zhentarim defenders as readily as the party. Destroying the rune drops the force field permanently — which may not be desirable if the party still has defenders to avoid on the way out.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:140: **K19 — Duhlark's Bedroom.** A flameskull patrols this room. A secret compartment beneath the floorboards (DC 14 Investigation) contains a wand of binding with 3 charges remaining — Duhlark's contingency item, missed by Manshoon's initial search because the flameskull's combat-mode activation damaged the floor when it first triggered on Manshoon's arrival.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:146: **E1 — Arrival Point.** The sanctum's receiving room. Glass walls show purple mist beyond. At Unaware and Suspicious tiers, E1 is empty — the sanctum is at normal operating posture. At Alert and Lockdown tiers, the simulacrum stands here. It looks exactly like Manshoon — same height, same robes, same silver hair, the same artificial left arm. It does not speak first. It evaluates.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:148: **E2 — Foyer.** 12 copper Masks of Manshoon mounted on the walls discharge 5 (1d10) lightning damage each when triggered by a Zhentarim coin pressure plate in the floor (DC 14 Perception to notice; DC 12 Thieves' Tools or DC 13 Arcana to disarm). The masks discharge simultaneously, targeting every creature in the room. This is the sanctum's entry alarm — the discharge also sends a magical pulse to E3, alerting Kaevja.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:150: **E3 — Guard Station.** Kaevja Cynavern (mage), Sidra's counterpart in the sanctum. Kaevja carries a yellow elemental gem (earth elemental) and has the sending spell prepared at all times. If she is not eliminated silently before she acts, her first action is to cast sending to Manshoon — regardless of his location — and to Agorn Fuoco or Vevette Blackwater in E8. A successful sending accelerates the reinforcement timer by 5 minutes. Kaevja is the sanctum's force multiplier: her sending is more dangerous than her combat capability. The party should treat her as a priority target.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:154: **E7 — Simulacrum Chamber.** Extremely cold; breath fogs at the door. An ice sarcophagus occupies the center of the room. At Unaware and Suspicious tiers, the simulacrum is here, dormant inside the sarcophagus — it activates automatically within 1 minute of Manshoon being incapacitated or killed. A party that reaches and defeats Manshoon in E13 without checking E7 may face a second Manshoon arriving behind them from this room without warning. At Alert and Lockdown tiers, the sarcophagus is empty — the simulacrum is already activated and standing in E1. The empty sarcophagus is readable: frost patterns inside show recent occupancy. A DC 13 Intelligence (Arcana) check identifies the sarcophagus as a simulacrum preservation vessel and concludes that the simulacrum has been recently removed, which means a duplicate is active somewhere in the sanctum.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:156: **E8 — Reading Room.** Vevette Blackwater + Agorn Fuoco (if survived **Faction Outposts**). Vevette fights; Agorn does not if the fight is going badly — he offers his ring (he carries one), Manshoon's daily routine, and the ledger's location in exchange for being allowed to leave. A green flame brazier in the center of the room: any creature saying "green flame" within 10 feet of the brazier while aware of its function triggers a fireball (8d6, DC 15 Dexterity saving throw). A DC 14 Investigation check on the brazier reveals the inscription. Manshoon told the lieutenants; Agorn will mention it if he is negotiating.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:158: **E9 — Library and Astral Vault.** The library is accessible normally. The vault at the back of the room is a bloodlocked mechanism: DC 20 Thieves' Tools to bypass with disadvantage, or DC 17 Arcana to bypass via dispel magic or disguise self (making the magical bloodsensor read the user as Manshoon). Inside: 3 spare pass-amulets, 2 spare teleporter rings, and Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game. The Report is the critical **Vault of Dragons** intelligence — it describes the Brandath Crypts approach, identifies Aurinax as "a guardian of considerable patience and considerable power," includes a partially decoded Elvish command phrase, and names Manshoon's analysis of all four faction positions in the Grand Game. This document goes directly to **Vault of Dragons** preparation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:160: **E10 — Audience Chamber.** Havia Quickknife + Mookie Plush, two halfling adepts who serve as Manshoon's household fanatics. Both fight to death. They are the sanctum's most physically capable non-spellcasting combatants — trained in martial arts, faster than their size suggests, and entirely loyal. They are also the least tactically sophisticated opponents in the sanctum; they attack in a straight line and do not retreat.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:162: **E12 — Manshoon's Quarters.** At Alert and Lockdown tiers, Manshoon is here when the party arrives — staff of power in hand, moving around the room with the controlled energy of someone who has been waiting for a fight. At Lockdown, he has cast mage armor, mind blank, and globe of invulnerability in the past hour. At Unaware and Suspicious, E12 is empty — Manshoon is in E13. A hidden staff compartment in the north wall (DC 16 Investigation) holds a spare arcane staff of ordinary make — not a staff of power, but a plausible decoy for a party who finds an empty room and decides to wait.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:164: Manshoon talks first. This is not a courtesy — he is assessing. He wants to know who sent the party, whether they have the Stone, whether the Vault approach has been compromised, and whether any of them represent a faction he might negotiate with. If the party has something he wants, he offers a trade. If they do not, he offers mercy: "Leave. Tell no one. This is the only time I will make this offer." If they refuse: he uses the simulacrum as a shield if it is present and activated, wall of force to isolate the most dangerous party member, dominate person on the second most dangerous. He does not commit to lethal combat until cornered. The Retributive Strike Gambit: at 30 HP or below, Manshoon grips the staff of power and states, clearly, that he will break it. This is not a bluff — a broken staff of power in E12 releases a 30-foot-radius force explosion dealing 8 × charges remaining force damage with a DC 17 Dexterity saving throw for half. The party must find a way to disarm him (DC 16 Sleight of Hand, a targeted attack to knock the staff from his grip, or a sufficiently compelling negotiation that takes him below the 30 HP threshold of despair) or accept the consequences.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:166: **E13 — Manshoon's Study.** At Unaware and Suspicious tiers, Manshoon is here — books open, the Stone of Golorr's history spread across the desk, notes in cramped Elvish describing the Vault's defenders and their known weaknesses. The party finds him at work. He reacts the same way he would in E12 — he talks first — but the setting is different: he is at a desk, not armed and waiting. He requires one action to reach the staff (on a weapon stand beside the desk) before the Retributive Strike Gambit becomes available. At Alert and Lockdown, E13 is empty. A helmed horror guards the spellbook shelf regardless of tier — it attacks any creature other than Manshoon who approaches the shelves. The blackmail ledger is in an arcane-locked drawer in the desk (DC 17 Thieves' Tools to bypass the lock; DC 15 Arcana to bypass via dispel magic or knock; knock works).
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:168: *Adversary Roster — Standard Configuration.* The full garrison is present in both towers and the sanctum. If Skeemo Weirdbottle survived **Gralhund Villa** or **Faction Outposts**, he is in the sanctum (the GM should note whether Skeemo escaped during those arcs). Skeemo recognizes the party and immediately alerts the garrison to their capabilities — he knows which party members are the primary threats, which spell slots were visible during their prior encounter, and which tactics worked against them before. His presence at Alert or Lockdown tiers means Manshoon has been briefed.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:175: | 5 min | 2 thugs arrive at K22 from the Interrogation House via the Yellowspire circle. Kaevja's sending reaches Manshoon and field agents if not silenced. |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:177: | 15 min | Manshoon (at Unaware/Suspicious, if not yet reached) moves from E13 to E12, retrieving the staff of power. The simulacrum (if dormant in E7) activates and moves to defend the sanctum. |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:179: | 25 min | If Manshoon was absent from the sanctum: his sending reply arrives. Simulacrum, if still operational, begins preparing final defensive position. |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:186: **Tone.** Violent and compressed. This is the campaign's most combat-intensive lair arc by design — the raid genre tests combat capability and speed, not social navigation or spatial puzzle-solving. The party that moves with purpose encounters a manageable garrison. The party that clears every room first, or that pauses to assess after each engagement, arrives at Manshoon facing a garrison that has had time to reinforce.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:192: **Purpose.** What goes wrong, the Doom Raiders parallel operation, the Manshoon confrontation and identity reveal, and extraction. The complications in **Kolat Towers** are not the rolling improvisation of **Sea Maidens Faire**'s caper structure — they are consequences of the raid's violence accumulating on both sides.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:198: If a PC is a Doom Raiders member, Tashlyn operates as a field ally. She briefs the party before the raid (Scene 3 coordination), her team enters via a different entry method than the party, and during Scene 4 she responds to the party's tactical situation with genuine cooperation — relaying garrison positions via hand signals if they encounter each other, pulling opposition off the party's flank, and prioritizing objectives that overlap with the party's (Manshoon's operational records, pass-amulet system destruction, Vevette Blackwater captured alive). If no PC is a DR member, the party discovers the Doom Raiders mid-raid — an independent force hitting the same target on the same night, with no coordination and no standing obligation to cooperate.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:204: | PCs in the sanctum | DR fight through towers toward K22 | DR may follow into the sanctum 10–15 minutes after party, arriving as reinforcements during Manshoon confrontation |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:205: | PCs defeated Manshoon, securing objectives | DR arrive, claim Manshoon's operational records | Negotiation over spoils; DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion) for full share |
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:209: *Facing Manshoon.* Regardless of which room the party reaches him in, Manshoon talks first. The conversation is an assessment — he wants to know what the party knows about the Vault, whether they have the Stone, and whether there is anything they want badly enough to trade for. His opening is never a threat; it is a question. "What do you actually want?" If the answer reveals that they have come for the ledger, he does not offer to trade it — that leverage is too important. If the answer reveals they have come for the Report on the Grand Game, he may be willing to discuss an arrangement, because the party having accurate Vault intelligence does not hurt him if he reaches the Vault first. If the answer reveals they have come to neutralize him entirely: "Then at least you're honest." Combat.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:211: Manshoon's combat priorities in order: the simulacrum as a shield (if present), wall of force to isolate the most dangerous party member, dominate person, and sustained concentration-based control rather than nova damage output. He is not trying to kill the party quickly — he is trying to exhaust them, because a fresh opponent makes better decisions than a desperate one. The Retributive Strike Gambit is real and available at 30 HP.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:213: *The Manshoon Identity Reveal.* This is **Kolat Towers**'s dramatic centerpiece. Three independent evidence trails converge:
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:215: Documents in E9 (Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game, which refers to "my predecessor" and "the original network" in a way that implies historical continuity without explaining it) + a sigil on documents in E13 (DC 14 Intelligence (History) to identify as the historical Zhentarim founder's personal mark, in use 200 years before the current Manshoon was born) + Lady Gondafrey's testimony, if she was freed ("He told me he founded the Zhentarim centuries ago. I thought he was mad. Then I saw what was left of his arm — the joint where it ends, the way it ends — and I stopped thinking he was mad.") combine to establish:
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:217: - Manshoon is a clone of a clone of one of the original Zhentarim founders, one of multiple surviving clone bodies.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:220: - The simulacrum mechanic: Manshoon can create ice-preserved duplicates with limited capabilities.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:221: - Manshoon's political reach extends to Masked Lords — his blackmail operation is decades in the making.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:223: No single piece is sufficient on its own. The party assembles the reveal from the documents they recover and the witnesses they find. A party that found Lady Gondafrey, read E13's documents, and identified the sigil has the full picture. A party that skipped K13 or didn't attempt E9's vault has a partial picture — enough to understand what Manshoon is, not enough to fully document it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:241: **Purpose.** Resolve the raid. No Eye to insert, no Stone upgrade. Instead: deliver the intelligence haul, mark the Manshoon Event Outcome, stage the identity reveal through faction debriefs, and position the party for **Vault of Dragons**. Scene 6 is shorter than in prior quests — the dramatic question of **Kolat Towers** resolves on a strategic rather than an emotional register. The party has done something violent and decisive; the aftermath reflects that.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:247: **The Blackmail Ledger (E13 desk).** Delivering it to Laeral Silverhand through Mirt or Vajra collapses Manshoon's political position — Corylus Thann and Jelenn Urmbrusk are exposed, their influence over Watch patrol assignments and Guildmaster votes evaporates, and two of Manshoon's most valuable civic tools are neutralized regardless of whether Manshoon himself survived. The identity list on the back pages names five Masked Lords total: Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk, Mirt, Thardouk Starbuckler, and Dorgar Adarbrent. Mirt will want a word with the party privately before the ledger goes to Laeral, not to suppress the names but to explain them: "I volunteered. The others didn't."
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:249: **Manshoon's Spellbook (E13 shelves, guarded by helmed horror).** A 9th-level wizard's working spellbook — 42 spells across all schools, with marginalia indicating active research into planar travel and gate mechanics. Vajra offers 15,000 gp and a favor from the Blackstaff's office in exchange for the spellbook, primarily for what its marginalia reveals about Manshoon's research trajectory. The Doom Raiders offer 8,000 gp and priority intelligence access. Jarlaxle offers, characteristically, "a conversation about the future" rather than a price — he is interested in the planar travel research for reasons he does not specify.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:251: **Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (E9 astral vault).** The most directly actionable **Vault of Dragons** item. Describes the Brandath Crypts approach in architectural detail, identifies Aurinax by name and species, includes a partially decoded Elvish command phrase (the party has the partial — the complete phrase will require additional research or the Stone's Full Awakening), and analyzes all four Grand Game factions' positions as of the report's date. The analysis is accurate. Manshoon's intelligence on the vault is as complete as anyone's outside the original Masked Lords who created it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:253: **Lady Gondafrey's Freedom (K13, if freed).** Gondafrey is not merely a rescued prisoner — she is a living witness who can testify before the Masked Lords Council about Manshoon's political infiltration, the blackmail operation's scope, and the simulacrum's nature. Her 113-year captivity is itself evidence. Savra will take Gondafrey to the Order's care immediately and arrange the testimony. Gondafrey herself has one request: to be present when the ledger is delivered to Laeral.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:255: *Manshoon Event Outcomes.*
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:257: Record one of three Manshoon Event Outcomes. Each is read directly in **Vault of Dragons**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:259: - **Manshoon Destroyed.** Both Manshoon (the clone of a clone) and the simulacrum were eliminated during the raid. The Splinter cell collapses within a tenday — Vevette (if alive) attempts to hold the surviving operatives together but lacks Manshoon's authority and his clone-network insurance. No Manshoon at the Vault. The party arrives at **Vault of Dragons** facing Aurinax without a third-party obstacle.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:260: - **Manshoon Simulacrum Only.** Manshoon was destroyed but the simulacrum survived and escaped (or was not found). The simulacrum has no 6th-level or higher spell slots and no staff of power. It arrives at the Vault with reduced capabilities and diminished tactical creativity — it can execute plans Manshoon taught it but cannot adapt to novel situations effectively.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:261: - **Manshoon Alive.** Manshoon survived (escaped, negotiated, or was never reached). He arrives at the Vault at full strength with surviving lieutenants and the staff of power. The hardest **Vault of Dragons** configuration. He will have had time to prepare.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:265: **Mirt (Harpers).** Wants the ledger, and specifically wants to review the identity list before Laeral receives it. "I volunteered" is the context he provides — he became a Masked Lord knowing Manshoon's network existed and chose to be legible to it rather than to fight it from cover. He is not apologizing. If the Manshoon Identity Reveal is fully assembled, Mirt is quiet for a moment before responding: "Clone of the Founder. Of course. That's why he could never be bargained with — he has nowhere to retreat to. He's already been everywhere."
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:267: **Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance).** "Is Manshoon neutralized?" Direct and operational. If **Manshoon Alive** is marked: "Then he will be at the Vault. We plan accordingly." If **Manshoon Destroyed** is marked: brief satisfaction, then immediately to the next question — the identity list and its implications for Watch patrol integrity. He will want to know which Watch officers Manshoon's Masked Lord contacts influenced.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:273: **Vajra Safahr (Force Grey).** "The spellbook. Tell me about the spellbook." Vajra's interest is less in the combat applications and more in the marginalia — Manshoon's planar travel research represents an intelligence gap Force Grey has been aware of for years. She also wants the construction specifications for the extradimensional sanctum: "A personal extradimensional space of that scale, anchored to a physical structure, is not a known construction technique. He developed it. I want to know how." If the party destroyed the sanctum without recovering the specifications, she is professionally disappointed.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:275: **Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders).** "Is it done?" If the DR participated in the raid, Tashlyn's debrief is businesslike — which objectives were achieved, which gaps remain, what the operational records recovered tell them about surviving Splinter personnel. If Vevette Blackwater was captured: "Good. She has things to answer for. So does the rest of what's left of that network." The Doom Raiders' internal war with the Manshoon Splinter ends here, one way or another. If the Splinter was only partially dismantled, Davil begins planning follow-up operations without asking the party's involvement.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:277: **Jarlaxle (if contacted in Scene 1).** If the Manshoon parallel strike fired in **Sea Maidens Faire** and the party helped or acknowledged it: "Now we're even. Or close enough." Jarlaxle wants to read the Report on the Grand Game — specifically Manshoon's analysis of BD's position in the Grand Game, which he suspects is more accurate than anyone outside BD has any right to be. If the party assembled the full Manshoon Identity Reveal: Jarlaxle confirms the one piece he withheld from his Scene 1 briefing. "I said I had suspicions. The arm was one. There is another thing about him — the way he holds the staff. No one born to a wand holds a staff like that. You hold it like something that has always been yours." He suspected. He wanted to see if the party could find the rest.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:281: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr with between 0 and 3 Eyes. If 3 Eyes are restored, **Vault of Dragons** launches — the Full Awakening has already occurred and the Vault's location is known; **Kolat Towers**'s intelligence confirms the approach route. If Eyes remain unrestored, other lair heists are still available; the intelligence haul from **Kolat Towers** (specifically Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game) enriches whichever quests follow. The Report's partial command phrase is a direct **Vault of Dragons** hook regardless of Eye count — the party knows the phrase is partially decoded and that completing it requires either the Stone's Full Awakening or additional research.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:283: **Tone.** Tactical decompression. **Kolat Towers**'s aftermath has a different texture than the prior lair heists' endings: no revelatory awakening (**Xanathar's Lair**), no moral reckoning (**Cassalanter Villa**), no relationship reassessment (**Sea Maidens Faire**). This is strategic positioning — the party has done a violent thing that was necessary, has recovered intelligence that changes the **Vault of Dragons** situation, and has marked a Manshoon Event Outcome that will determine what they face at the Vault. Professional satisfaction or grim assessment, depending on how the operation went.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:307: **Kolat Towers**'s transition is not open-ended in the same way as prior lair heists. The remaining task is **Vault of Dragons**. The intelligence haul — Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game, the Blackmail Ledger, the partial command phrase — feeds directly into **Vault of Dragons** preparation. If the party completes **Kolat Towers** with all three Eyes restored, **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:313: - **Faction Outposts**, Scene 4 (Manshoon Outposts) — entry methods seeded here (Yellowspire circle, pass-amulets). Doom Raiders Missions 5–6 produce the ledger and gap diagram.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:317: - **Sea Maidens Faire**, J30 (Jarlaxle's Report) — references Manshoon's operations. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:318: - **Sea Maidens Faire**, Scene 5 (Zhentarim Strike Team) — pass-amulets recoverable from defeated Zhentarim. Entry method for **Kolat Towers**. Jarlaxle's "They came to my ship" motivation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:319: - **Vault of Dragons** — the Manshoon Event Outcome (**Manshoon Destroyed**, **Manshoon Simulacrum Only** or **Manshoon Alive**) + intelligence haul (Report on the Grand Game, partial command phrase) directly shape the Vault approach.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:320: - **Notable Figures** — Existing profiles: Manshoon, Sidra Romeir, Manafret Cherryport, Vevette Blackwater, Agorn Fuoco, Urstul Floxin, Lady Gondafrey, Skeemo Weirdbottle. New profiles needed: Kaevja Cynavern, Havia Quickknife, Mookie Plush, Yorn the Terror.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:321: - **the Bestiary (not yet drafted)** — Boss stat block needed: Manshoon (with simulacrum mechanic distinction). Standard stat blocks needed: Havia Quickknife, Mookie Plush.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:324: - **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) — Manshoon faction overview, escalation framework, Kolat Towers lair entry (Southern Ward).
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:327: - **Undermountain (future)** — Manshoon's Halaster connection seeds Level 8 content. The Kolat Brothers' extradimensional sanctum construction technique seeds Dweomercore on Level 9.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:339: Four quests, four genres, because the party has four different capability sets that a single-genre sequence cannot test. The raid placement at the end of the sequence is deliberate: by **Kolat Towers**, the party has demonstrated stealth capability (**Xanathar's Lair**), social intelligence (**Cassalanter Villa**), and improvisational flexibility (**Sea Maidens Faire**). The raid asks whether they can leverage all of those in a context where they cannot afford to use any of them thoughtfully — speed collapses the time for planning, and the reinforcement timer punishes hesitation. A party that treats the raid like a dungeon crawl (clearing every room before advancing) loses to the timer. A party that treats it like a caper (improvising room by room without a plan) arrives at Manshoon underpowered. The raid rewards parties who have internalized the prior quests' lessons and can execute without pausing to plan.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:343: **Kolat Towers** is the only lair heist without an Eye recovery. The structural risk is that the scene feels lower-stakes than **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, or **Sea Maidens Faire** — that without the Stone's awakening, the climax lacks weight. The intelligence haul addresses this by giving each recovered item a specific, named **Vault of Dragons** consequence that the party understands before they enter the towers. The blackmail ledger is not "valuable treasure" — it is the political tool that neutralizes Manshoon's Masked Lord influence. The Report on the Grand Game is not "lore" — it contains the partial command phrase that opens the Vault. Every item in the haul has a downstream consequence that the party can predict, which makes recovering it feel as meaningful as restoring an Eye.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:345: The Manshoon Identity Reveal replaces the Stone's awakening as the quest's dramatic centerpiece. Where the Stone awakening in prior quests revealed something about the campaign's hidden architecture (Nihiloor's domination in **Xanathar's Lair**, the Cassalanters' debt in **Cassalanter Villa**, the aboleth consciousness in **Sea Maidens Faire**), the Identity Reveal reveals something about the antagonist's nature that recontextualizes every Manshoon interaction the party has had since **Faction Outposts**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:349: The simulacrum's predecided positioning creates two structurally different encounter sequences depending on escalation tier. At Alert and Lockdown, the party encounters the simulacrum first in E1 — they may believe they have defeated Manshoon before they reach E12. At Unaware and Suspicious, the party reaches Manshoon first in E13 and may not discover the simulacrum in E7 until it activates behind them.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:351: Three discoverable clues let the party identify which version they fought: the simulacrum cannot cast 6th-level or higher spells (DC 15 Arcana to notice the pattern after observing its spell selection); the simulacrum has roughly half the hit points of the original (a party that tracked damage dealt will notice the fight ended faster than expected); and Lady Gondafrey, if freed, knows that Manshoon "sometimes sends a quieter version of himself" and can describe the behavioral difference after the fact.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:353: The ambiguity is a puzzle, not a gotcha. The clues are present and discoverable. A party that notices and investigates walks out of the sanctum knowing exactly what they fought. A party that does not notice and investigate may discover the answer in the **Vault of Dragons** confrontation instead — when they arrive at the Vault expecting a diminished Manshoon and encounter a full-strength one.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:361: ## The Manshoon Identity Reveal
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:363: The three-quest reveal structure — Name in **Faction Outposts**, Identity in **Kolat Towers**, Motivation in **Vault of Dragons** — treats Manshoon differently than the other three villain faction leaders. Xanathar is immediately legible; Jarlaxle makes himself legible on his own terms; the Cassalanters are legible once the party reaches their temple. Manshoon is the one faction leader whose true nature requires assembly from distributed evidence, because his true nature is genuinely strange — a clone of a 200-year-old wizard, not just a Zhentarim leader — and because understanding it changes the party's strategic relationship with him in a way that understanding the other faction leaders does not.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:365: A party that assembles the full reveal understands why Manshoon cannot negotiate the ledger away (he cannot expose the operation without exposing himself), why he fled Undermountain (Halaster is one of the few beings who could destroy him permanently), and why his Vault ambitions are less about the gold than about the political legitimacy 500,000 dragons would purchase. This is the Motivation that **Vault of Dragons** will confirm.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:369: Each lair heist has a moment that asks the party to weigh a consequence against a tactical advantage: smokepowder demolition in **Xanathar's Lair**, the children's sacrifice in **Cassalanter Villa**, Zelifarn's crystal ball in **Sea Maidens Faire**. **Kolat Towers**'s equivalent is the Retributive Strike Gambit. If the party is winning the fight against Manshoon, they must decide how to prevent him from breaking the staff — which means either disarming him while he's still dangerous enough to threaten the gambit, or accepting the explosion, or finding a third path (negotiation that takes him past his desperation threshold without combat reaching 30 HP). The gambit compresses **Kolat Towers**'s moral dimension into a single decision point because the raid genre does not leave space for extended moral deliberation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:373: The escalation tier system reaches its harshest expression in **Kolat Towers**. Lockdown tier halves the timer (30 to 15 minutes), seals the force-field gaps, invalidates captured amulets, and pre-activates the simulacrum. A party that hit every Manshoon outpost in **Faction Outposts** arrives at Kolat Towers with Lockdown already active — which means the fastest timer, the fewest entry options, and the best-prepared garrison. This is the correct consequence of aggressive **Faction Outposts** play. The difficulty is not a punishment; it is a calibration. The party that did the most to expose and destabilize the Splinter network is also the party most capable of running the raid under the hardest conditions.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:19: As the Stone's third Eye clicks into its socket — in whichever lair delivered it — every surviving player in the Grand Game receives the same piece of information at the same time: the vault is real, it is beneath the Brandath Mausoleum, and it can now be opened. Xanathar, if it survives, prefers the gold stay buried and the Stone stay lost — a vault it cannot reach is still leverage it can threaten to use. The Cassalanters, depending on how the party left them, either wait on a promise or plan to take by force what they were once willing to earn. Manshoon, who holds no Eye, has been counting on exactly this moment — he intends to let someone else do the work of opening the vault and then take it from whoever succeeds. And in Neverwinter, word of a Waterdeep in open faction war over a half-million gold pieces has already reached a man who has been waiting years for a reason to come home. Dagult Neverember is packing.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:34: | Manshoon | **Kolat Towers** Scene 6 (if run) | **Manshoon Destroyed** / **Manshoon Simulacrum Only** / **Manshoon Alive**. If **Kolat Towers** was never run, treat as **Manshoon Alive**, undiminished, and unidentified — the party has not yet assembled the Identity Reveal. |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:45: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* If Lords' Alliance Mission 6 succeeded, Jalester delivers the Masked Lord's Writ's practical terms one final time: the Vault district gate opens to it, Watch officers defer to it within four rounds of presentation, and Laeral has one legal intervention available during **Vault of Dragons** — usable once, for a rival faction complication, a legal challenge, or an NPC arrest. If the party argued Laeral out of the Manshoon leverage offer, Jalester confirms it: "He has no card to play with her. Whatever he tries at the vault, it won't be that." If the offer was accepted instead, Jalester warns that Manshoon can still delay Watch cooperation for up to two hours, or threaten to expose a Masked Lord publicly, if he reaches the vault able to make good on it. At renown 10+, Jalester personally escorts the party to the Vault district gate and stands with them through the Watch's initial questioning, cutting what would otherwise be a lengthy procedural delay down to minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:53: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* If Doom Raiders Mission 6 ("Ziraj's Last Hunt") succeeded, Davil's final note to the party — the one listing every identified Splinter operative "for **Kolat Towers**" — has already done its work if **Kolat Towers** was run. Here, the contact's business is the gold itself. Davil states the Doom Raiders' position without apology: "We're not asking you to give us anything. We're telling you what we intend to ask for, once the vault is open." At Dread Lord renown, Davil proposes a 10% split of the recovered gold in exchange for concrete operational support: a distraction crew, a locksmith backup, and a wagon extraction team for the gold itself. If Skeemo Weirdbottle survived and **Manshoon Simulacrum Only** is marked, Skeemo is not with the Doom Raiders today — Tashlyn confirms, grimly, that he is at the vault working for the simulacrum, and will recognize the party on sight. At renown 10+, Tashlyn commits a four-person Doom Raiders team to the City of the Dead operation, coordinated on the same terms as **Kolat Towers** if that quest was run, or offered fresh if it wasn't.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:78: - Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (**Kolat Towers**, area E9) — if the party secured the contents of the bloodlocked vault during the Kolat Towers raid, this document describes the Brandath Crypts approach and identifies the mausoleum by name. A party that completed **Kolat Towers** and extracted this intelligence enters **Vault of Dragons** already knowing the route.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:118: - **Sunlight.** The *daylight* spell suffices — its light counts as sunlight under 2024 rules, and at 3rd level it's well within reach of a 7th-level party. So does Manshoon's sunglobe, if **Manshoon Destroyed** is marked and the item was recovered from Kolat Towers. Absent either, twenty large steel mirrors (400 gp total) and several hours of careful alignment work will do.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:126: - Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (**Kolat Towers**, area E9) — the same document that names the Brandath Crypts includes a partial account of the vault-opening ceremony alongside a partially decoded Elvish command phrase. A party that extracted this intelligence in **Kolat Towers** enters Scene 3 with the ceremony's outline already in hand; the command phrase is usable, if incomplete, and reduces the work of sourcing the remaining details.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:194: | Manshoon's Splinter | **Manshoon Destroyed** is not marked | See Manshoon's Three Outcomes below | Seize the gold and the Stone; recover the dragonstaff if possible |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:206: **Manshoon's Three Outcomes.** Reused directly from **Kolat Towers** Scene 6, and the single most consequential row in this table.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:208: - **Manshoon Destroyed.** Absent. No Splinter obstacle at the vault.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:209: - **Manshoon Simulacrum Only.** Present but diminished — no spell slots above 5th level, no staff of power, capable of executing plans Manshoon taught it but not of adapting to a situation he never anticipated.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:210: - **Manshoon Alive** (or **Kolat Towers** not run). Full strength: staff of power in hand, surviving lieutenants at his side, and — if he reached the vault first through his own intelligence network — the advantage of having had time to prepare a position rather than react to the party's arrival. This is the hardest **Vault of Dragons** configuration, and the default one if **Kolat Towers** was skipped.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:212: If more than one hostile faction is present simultaneously, they do not coordinate with each other — Manshoon, Xanathar's remnants, and an Active-Adversarial Cassalanter team are all here for the same gold and are as much a threat to one another as to the party. A party that recognizes this can let its enemies do some of the fighting for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:214: *Facing Manshoon (If **Manshoon Alive** or **Manshoon Simulacrum Only** Is Marked).* He talks first, exactly as he did in **Kolat Towers** if that confrontation occurred. His question here is not "what do you want" — he already knows. It is closer to: "You've done all the work. Why not let someone experienced handle what comes next?" He is not bluffing about being willing to fight for the gold, but he would still rather not — a fight this public, this close to the city's attention, is exactly the kind of exposure he has spent years avoiding. A party that can make continued exposure feel more costly to him than walking away may end this without combat at all.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:218: *The Dragonstaff's Stakes.* Whoever holds the dragonstaff of Ahghairon after Aurinax's oath is discharged decides which dragons the ward admits, one touched creature at a time. This is not a minor detail to gloss past in Scene 6's gold logistics — it is the reason Jarlaxle wants it, the reason Manshoon would take it if he could, and the reason Dagult, whatever else he's lost, still believes he has some claim on it. If the party is deciding what to do with the staff, this is the moment that decision's stakes should be made explicit to them.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:272: - **Halaster notices.** Within days of the vault's opening, a cryptic message reaches the party — a flameskull messenger, a fragment of dream, a brief and unsettling projection. Halaster Blackcloak, who has taken a professional interest in Manshoon before, has now taken one in the party as well.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:295: A party can only reach **Vault of Dragons** with at least three lair heists done, since all three Eyes (from **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**) are required to open the Vault — Kolat Towers carries no Eye and is never required for entry, only for the fourth heist slot that unlocks level 7 early. This is why the quest is written for a 6th-to-7th-level party rather than a fixed 7th — Scene 5's Faction Confrontation in particular should be run with the entering level in mind (see that scene's roster-assembly table), since a level 6 party that skipped Kolat Towers also faces a full-strength, unidentified Manshoon by default (**Kolat Towers** is what typically resolves that threat).
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:315: *Transition.* There is no further quest within Dragon Heist to transition into. What follows, if the table continues, is Undermountain. 8th level is this campaign's intended handoff point into it — the outcome for a party that did all four heists — but a party that skipped Kolat Towers descends at 7th instead, having traded the deeper start for whatever they gained by leaving Manshoon's fortress alone. Either is a valid place to begin Dungeon of the Mad Mage; the difference is a consequence of the party's own choices, not a gap to patch. The five Mad Mage bridge hooks in Scene 6 are the GM's starting toolkit for that transition — use as many or as few as the table wants, but do not feel obligated to use all five. A campaign can end cleanly here, with nothing left unresolved that the party didn't choose to leave open.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:326: - **Kolat Towers** Scene 6 — the Manshoon Event Outcomes (**Manshoon Destroyed**, **Manshoon Simulacrum Only**, **Manshoon Alive**) and the full Manshoon Identity Reveal, both read directly in Scene 5.
